=== quad.ts ===
/*
 * Mapping the replicated app screen onto the monitor in the generated plate.
 *
 * The quad below was DETECTED from public/plates/room-v3.png, not eyeballed:
 * the screen is the largest light, low-saturation region in the image, and its
 * four extreme corners were taken from that mask. Left edge 1936px, right edge
 * 1668px, so the monitor is turned 13.8% away from the camera. Any new plate
 * must be re-detected, never adjusted by hand.
 */

export type Pt = readonly [number, number];

/**
 * Screen corners in room-v3.png, normalised 0..1. TL, TR, BR, BL.
 *
 * Pushed out from the detected centroid by OVERSCAN. The detector finds the
 * light region, which stops a pixel or two inside the bezel, and the first
 * render showed a grey sliver of the original plate down the right edge. The
 * composite must overshoot into the bezel, never fall short of it.
 */
const DETECTED: readonly [Pt, Pt, Pt, Pt] = [
  [0.0712, 0.1198],
  [0.6156, 0.1839],
  [0.6052, 0.7266],
  [0.0752, 0.7500],
] as const;

const OVERSCAN = 1.042;

export const SCREEN_QUAD: readonly [Pt, Pt, Pt, Pt] = (() => {
  const cx = DETECTED.reduce((a, p) => a + p[0], 0) / 4;
  const cy = DETECTED.reduce((a, p) => a + p[1], 0) / 4;
  return DETECTED.map(
    ([x, y]) => [cx + (x - cx) * OVERSCAN, cy + (y - cy) * OVERSCAN] as Pt,
  ) as unknown as readonly [Pt, Pt, Pt, Pt];
})();

/** The plate is 5504x3072 (1.792:1), slightly wider than the 16:9 frame. */
export const PLATE_W = 5504;
export const PLATE_H = 3072;

/**
 * CSS matrix3d that maps an axis-aligned w x h element onto an arbitrary quad.
 *
 * Solves the projective transform taking the unit square to (p0,p1,p2,p3),
 * then post-multiplies by scale(1/w, 1/h) so the element's own pixel size is
 * absorbed. Requires transformOrigin "0 0" on the element.
 */
export function quadTransform(
  w: number,
  h: number,
  p0: Pt, p1: Pt, p2: Pt, p3: Pt,
): string {
  const [x0, y0] = p0, [x1, y1] = p1, [x2, y2] = p2, [x3, y3] = p3;

  const dx1 = x1 - x2, dx2 = x3 - x2, sx = x0 - x1 + x2 - x3;
  const dy1 = y1 - y2, dy2 = y3 - y2, sy = y0 - y1 + y2 - y3;

  const den = dx1 * dy2 - dx2 * dy1;
  const g = den === 0 ? 0 : (sx * dy2 - dx2 * sy) / den;
  const hh = den === 0 ? 0 : (dx1 * sy - sx * dy1) / den;

  const a = x1 - x0 + g * x1;
  const b = x3 - x0 + hh * x3;
  const c = x0;
  const d = y1 - y0 + g * y1;
  const e = y3 - y0 + hh * y3;
  const f = y0;

  // columns divided by the element's own dimensions
  const m = [
    a / w, d / w, 0, g / w,
    b / h, e / h, 0, hh / h,
    0, 0, 1, 0,
    c, f, 0, 1,
  ];
  return `matrix3d(${m.map((n) => Number(n.toFixed(6))).join(",")})`;
}

/** The quad in frame pixels, for a frame of the given size (plate covers it). */
export function screenQuadPx(frameW: number, frameH: number): [Pt, Pt, Pt, Pt] {
  // The plate is scaled to COVER the frame, then centred.
  const scale = Math.max(frameW / PLATE_W, frameH / PLATE_H);
  const drawnW = PLATE_W * scale, drawnH = PLATE_H * scale;
  const offX = (frameW - drawnW) / 2, offY = (frameH - drawnH) / 2;
  return SCREEN_QUAD.map(
    ([nx, ny]) => [offX + nx * drawnW, offY + ny * drawnH] as Pt,
  ) as [Pt, Pt, Pt, Pt];
}

=== Room.tsx ===
import React from "react";
import { AbsoluteFill, Img, staticFile, interpolate, Easing } from "remotion";
import { SEC, LOGICAL_W, LOGICAL_H } from "../rrc/tokens";
import { quadTransform, screenQuadPx } from "./quad";
import type { Chunk } from "../rrc/Stimulus";
import type { Seg, Phase } from "./beats";
import { phaseAt, phaseProgress, returnAt } from "./beats";

/*
 * The POV room.
 *
 * Plate, screen and every floating card live inside ONE camera wrapper, so the
 * whole world moves together. That is what makes an annotation read as an
 * object in the room rather than an overlay on the frame.
 *
 * Two rules this file exists to keep:
 *  1. The screen is Remotion, never generated. Kling cannot render the stimulus.
 *  2. Nothing marks the text. Attention is shown with FOCUS, because focus is a
 *     property of the eye and cannot be mistaken for an app feature.
 */

/** How far the eye slides off the text when he looks away, in frame px. */
const GLANCE_X = 96;
const GLANCE_Y = 26;
/**
 * Push in far enough that panning never exposes the plate's edge, and NO
 * further, because zoom crops the monitor.
 *
 * The monitor spans roughly x 136..1190 of an unzoomed frame. Scaling about the
 * centre by z puts its left edge at 960 + (136 - 960) * z, so z = 1.30 pushed it
 * to -111 and cropped the app's own footer instruction off the screen. In an
 * instructional film, cropping the thing being read is the wrong trade.
 *
 * At z = 1.12 the left edge sits at +37, and the widest safe translate is
 * (z - 1) / 2 * 1920 = 115, which comfortably clears a 96px glance plus 6px of
 * drift. The glance is smaller than it was and still unmistakable, because the
 * monitor visibly slides out of the reading position.
 */
const BASE_ZOOM = 1.12;

const ease = Easing.bezier(0.4, 0, 0.2, 1);

export function cameraAt(s: Seg, t: number): { x: number; y: number; zoom: number } {
  const p = phaseAt(s, t);
  const k = phaseProgress(s, t);

  if (p === "read") {
    // Micro-drift down the Chunk, the way an eye tracks a line. Deliberately
    // small: this should be felt, not seen.
    return { x: -6 * k, y: 7 * k, zoom: BASE_ZOOM + 0.006 * k };
  }
  if (p === "release") {
    // Focus lets go before the eye does.
    return { x: interpolate(k, [0, 1], [-6, -26], { easing: ease }),
             y: interpolate(k, [0, 1], [7, 12], { easing: ease }),
             zoom: BASE_ZOOM + 0.006 };
  }
  if (p === "speak") {
    // Glanced off. Settles quickly, then hangs with a slow living drift.
    const settle = interpolate(k, [0, 0.14], [0, 1], { extrapolateRight: "clamp", easing: ease });
    const drift = Math.sin(k * Math.PI * 1.6) * 6;
    return { x: -26 - (GLANCE_X - 26) * settle + drift,
             y: 12 + (GLANCE_Y - 12) * settle + drift * 0.35,
             zoom: BASE_ZOOM + 0.006 - 0.012 * settle };
  }
  // confirm: the eye comes back to the page
  const back = interpolate(k, [0, 0.42], [0, 1], { extrapolateRight: "clamp", easing: ease });
  return { x: interpolate(back, [0, 1], [-GLANCE_X, 0]),
           y: interpolate(back, [0, 1], [GLANCE_Y, 0]),
           zoom: interpolate(back, [0, 1], [BASE_ZOOM - 0.006, BASE_ZOOM]) };
}

/** Blur applied to a Chunk, in px. Zero only for the Chunk being read. */
export function chunkBlur(active: boolean, phase: Phase, k: number): number {
  if (phase === "read") return active ? 0 : 2.6;
  if (phase === "release") return active ? interpolate(k, [0, 1], [0, 2.2]) : 2.6;
  if (phase === "speak") return 3.1;              // he is not reading any of it
  return interpolate(k, [0, 0.42], [3.1, 0.9], { extrapolateRight: "clamp" });
}

/** The stimulus as the drill renders it, with optical defocus and no marking. */
export const POVStimulus: React.FC<{
  chunks: Chunk[];
  activeNs: number[];
  phase: Phase;
  k: number;
}> = ({ chunks, activeNs, phase, k }) => (
  <div
    style={{
      maxWidth: "52ch", color: SEC.ink, fontFamily: SEC.serif,
      fontSize: SEC.stimulusPx, fontWeight: 400, lineHeight: SEC.stimulusLh,
      fontOpticalSizing: "none", fontVariationSettings: '"opsz" 14',
    }}
  >
    {chunks.map((c) => {
      const on = activeNs.includes(c.n);
      const b = chunkBlur(on, phase, k);
      return (
        <span
          key={c.n}
          style={{
            filter: b > 0.02 ? `blur(${b.toFixed(2)}px)` : "none",
            opacity: b > 0.02 ? 0.62 : 1,
            display: "inline",
          }}
        >
          {c.text}{" "}
        </span>
      );
    })}
  </div>
);

/** Composites any screen content onto the monitor in the plate. */
export const Monitor: React.FC<{
  frameW: number; frameH: number; children: React.ReactNode;
}> = ({ frameW, frameH, children }) => {
  const [p0, p1, p2, p3] = screenQuadPx(frameW, frameH);
  return (
    <div
      style={{
        position: "absolute", top: 0, left: 0,
        width: LOGICAL_W, height: LOGICAL_H,
        transform: quadTransform(LOGICAL_W, LOGICAL_H, p0, p1, p2, p3),
        transformOrigin: "0 0",
        // The panel is emissive; a touch of light spills past its edges.
        boxShadow: "0 0 120px 24px rgba(150,170,255,0.10)",
      }}
    >
      {children}
    </div>
  );
};

export const Room: React.FC<{
  seg: Seg; t: number; frameW: number; frameH: number;
  /* Beats that are not part of the read/glance loop supply their own camera.
     During The Recall he is facing a blank screen, not glancing off it, so
     inheriting the glance-return would crop the app's own footer. */
  cam?: { x: number; y: number; zoom: number };
  children: React.ReactNode;
}> = ({ seg, t, frameW, frameH, cam: camOverride, children }) => {
  const cam = camOverride ?? cameraAt(seg, t);
  return (
    <AbsoluteFill style={{ overflow: "hidden", background: "#0b0a10" }}>
      <div
        style={{
          position: "absolute", inset: 0,
          transform: `translate(${cam.x.toFixed(2)}px, ${cam.y.toFixed(2)}px) scale(${cam.zoom.toFixed(4)})`,
          transformOrigin: "50% 50%",
        }}
      >
        <Img
          src={staticFile("plates/room-v3.png")}
          style={{ position: "absolute", inset: 0, width: "100%", height: "100%", objectFit: "cover" }}
        />
        {children}
      </div>
    </AbsoluteFill>
  );
};

export { returnAt };

/** Neutral: squarely on the screen, nothing panned. */
export const CAM_NEUTRAL = { x: 0, y: 0, zoom: BASE_ZOOM } as const;

=== POVInstructional.tsx ===
import React from "react";
import { AbsoluteFill, Audio, staticFile, useCurrentFrame, useVideoConfig, interpolate } from "remotion";
import { Screen } from "../rrc/Screen";
import type { Chunk } from "../rrc/Stimulus";
import { Room, Monitor, POVStimulus, CAM_NEUTRAL } from "./Room";
import { SpeechBubble, enterAt } from "./Float";
import { StepCard, StepLabel, type StepKey } from "./Instruction";
import type { Seg, Phase } from "./beats";

export type Cue = { start: number; end: number; text: string };

export type Beat = {
  kind: "card" | "read" | "release" | "speak" | "press" | "recall" | "settle";
  start: number;
  end: number;
  step?: StepKey;
  gi?: number;
  chunkNs?: number[];
  /* Where this beat sits in the ORIGINAL recording, so caption cues still
     resolve once teaching beats have been cut into the timeline. */
  srcStart?: number;
  phase?: string;
};

export type Script = { slug: string; duration: number; beats: Beat[] };

const mmss = (t: number) =>
  `${Math.floor(t / 60)}:${String(Math.floor(t % 60)).padStart(2, "0")}`;

/*
 * The instructional cut.
 *
 * Driven entirely by the beat script that align/build_script.py emitted
 * alongside the soundtrack, so picture and audio cannot drift.
 *
 *   card     the picture holds and a step is introduced. His audio is silent.
 *   read     SILENT. The eye tracks the chunk, everything else is soft.
 *   release  focus lets go before the eye does.
 *   speak    he has glanced off; what he says arrives beside him.
 *   press    the app's own Space chip depresses.
 *   recall   the real drill exactly: paper stays, text gone.
 */
export const POVInstructional: React.FC<{
  slug: string;
  chunks: Chunk[];
  script: Script;
  cues: Cue[];
  audioSrc: string;
  where: string;
  qIndex: number;
  qTotal: number;
}> = ({ chunks, script, cues, audioSrc, where, qIndex, qTotal }) => {
  const frame = useCurrentFrame();
  const { fps, width, height, durationInFrames } = useVideoConfig();
  const t = frame / fps;

  const beat = script.beats.find((b) => t >= b.start && t < b.end) ?? script.beats[script.beats.length - 1];
  const k = beat.end > beat.start ? (t - beat.start) / (beat.end - beat.start) : 1;

  // Which step is on screen. Cards name themselves; the rest map from the beat.
  const stepOf: Partial<Record<Beat["kind"], StepKey>> = {
    read: "read", release: "tell", speak: "colour", press: "recall", recall: "recall",
  };
  const step: StepKey | null = beat.kind === "card" ? beat.step! : (stepOf[beat.kind] ?? null);

  // A step is only labelled once its card has played.
  const introduced = new Set(
    script.beats.filter((b) => b.kind === "card" && b.start < t).map((b) => b.step!),
  );
  const anyCards = script.beats.some((b) => b.kind === "card");
  const labelOn = step !== null && beat.kind !== "card" && (!anyCards || introduced.has(step));

  // Frames since this beat began, for springing the label in.
  const beatFrames = Math.round((t - beat.start) * fps);

  // The stimulus is gone from the press onward, exactly as the drill does it.
  const recalling = beat.kind === "recall" || beat.kind === "settle" ||
    (beat.kind === "press" && k > 0.55);
  const pressed = beat.kind === "press"
    ? interpolate(k, [0.5, 0.58, 0.72, 0.8], [0, 1, 1, 0], { extrapolateLeft: "clamp", extrapolateRight: "clamp" })
    : 0;

  // Cue lookup runs in ORIGINAL recording time, because the cues were written
  // against his real run, and srcStart maps this beat back onto it.
  const srcT = beat.srcStart !== undefined ? beat.srcStart + (t - beat.start) : -1;
  const cueIdx = srcT >= 0 ? cues.findIndex((c) => srcT >= c.start && srcT < c.end) : -1;
  const cue = cueIdx >= 0 ? cues[cueIdx] : null;
  const showBubble = (beat.kind === "speak" || beat.kind === "release") && !!cue;

  // A synthetic segment so the existing focus logic keeps working unchanged.
  const activeNs = beat.chunkNs ?? [];
  const phase: Phase =
    beat.kind === "read" ? "read" :
    beat.kind === "release" ? "release" :
    beat.kind === "speak" ? "speak" : "confirm";
  const seg: Seg = {
    gi: beat.gi ?? 0, chunkNs: activeNs,
    readStart: beat.start, readEnd: beat.kind === "read" ? beat.end : beat.start,
    saidStart: beat.kind === "read" ? beat.end : beat.start,
    workEnd: beat.end, readCoverage: 1,
  };

  const through = Math.min(1, frame / durationInFrames);

  return (
    <AbsoluteFill>
      <Audio src={staticFile(audioSrc)} />

      <Room
        seg={seg}
        t={t}
        frameW={width}
        frameH={height}
        cam={["press", "recall", "settle", "card"].includes(beat.kind) ? CAM_NEUTRAL : undefined}
      >
        <Monitor frameW={width} frameH={height}>
          <Screen
            through={through}
            clock={mmss(t)}
            where={`${where}  ·  ${qIndex} of ${qTotal}`}
            instruction={recalling
              ? "Say the whole argument back from memory."
              : "Read it. Tell it back and colour it in as you go."}
            keyDoes={recalling ? "next stimulus" : "recall full stimulus"}
            pressed={pressed}
          >
            {recalling ? null : (
              <POVStimulus chunks={chunks} activeNs={activeNs} phase={phase} k={k} />
            )}
          </Screen>
        </Monitor>

        {showBubble && cue && (
          <SpeechBubble
            key={cueIdx}
            text={cue.text}
            enter={enterAt(Math.round((srcT - cue.start) * fps), fps)}
            x={1120}
            y={392}
          />
        )}

        {labelOn && step && (
          <StepLabel step={step} enter={enterAt(beatFrames, fps)} />
        )}
      </Room>

      {beat.kind === "card" && beat.step && <StepCard step={beat.step} k={k} />}
    </AbsoluteFill>
  );
};
=== beats.ts ===
/*
 * The loop, as data.
 *
 * Every beat below comes from public/content/timeline.json, which was produced
 * by global Needleman-Wunsch alignment of walkthrough_md against the Whisper
 * word stream. Nothing here is authored timing; it is his real run.
 *
 *   readStart -> readEnd    he is reading the Chunk IN HIS HEAD
 *   readEnd   -> saidStart  the release: he stops reading, before he speaks
 *   saidStart -> returnAt   he has glanced off and is speaking out loud
 *   returnAt  -> workEnd    his eye comes back and what he carried is confirmed
 */

export type Seg = {
  gi: number;
  chunkNs: number[];
  readStart: number;
  readEnd: number;
  saidStart: number;
  workEnd: number;
  readCoverage: number;
};

export type Phase = "read" | "release" | "speak" | "confirm";

/** How long the eye-returns-and-confirms beat runs, in seconds. */
export const CONFIRM_S = 3.2;

export function returnAt(s: Seg): number {
  // Never eat more than a third of the speaking stretch.
  const speakLen = s.workEnd - s.saidStart;
  return s.workEnd - Math.min(CONFIRM_S, speakLen / 3);
}

export function phaseAt(s: Seg, t: number): Phase {
  if (t < s.readEnd) return "read";
  if (t < s.saidStart) return "release";
  if (t < returnAt(s)) return "speak";
  return "confirm";
}

export function segAt(segments: Seg[], t: number): Seg {
  for (const s of segments) if (t < s.workEnd) return s;
  return segments[segments.length - 1];
}

/** 0..1 through the current phase, for driving camera and focus. */
export function phaseProgress(s: Seg, t: number): number {
  const p = phaseAt(s, t);
  const span =
    p === "read" ? [s.readStart, s.readEnd] :
    p === "release" ? [s.readEnd, s.saidStart] :
    p === "speak" ? [s.saidStart, returnAt(s)] :
    [returnAt(s), s.workEnd];
  const [a, b] = span;
  if (b <= a) return 1;
  return Math.min(1, Math.max(0, (t - a) / (b - a)));
}

=== build_script.py ===
"""Build the instructional cut: one audio file and one picture timeline, together.

Round 5 turned this from a continuous playback of his run into his run with
teaching cut into it. Picture and audio must therefore be built from the SAME
script or they drift, so this emits both.

The rules it encodes, all decided with him:
  R5-Q2  reading is SILENT. His voice is muted across every read span, and the
         span keeps its real duration so the reading PACE stays visible.
  R5-Q1  each step is introduced with a full stop the first time it appears,
         and only labelled thereafter.
  R5-Q3  the labels are Reading / Tell it back / Colouring In, the app's own
         student-facing words.
  R5-Q5  The Recall is the real drill exactly: Space, text gone, say it back.

Output per stimulus:
  public/audio/<slug>.instr.mp3     the built soundtrack
  public/content/<slug>.script.json the picture timeline, frame-accurate
"""
import json, io, os, subprocess, sys

BASE = os.path.dirname(os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__)))
TL = json.load(io.open(os.path.join(BASE, "public/content/timeline.json"), encoding="utf-8"))
AUD = os.path.join(BASE, "public/audio")
GEN = os.path.join(BASE, "recall/generated")
TMP = os.path.join(BASE, "align", "_instr")
os.makedirs(TMP, exist_ok=True)

# How long each introduction card holds, in seconds.
HOLD = {"read": 5.0, "tell": 5.4, "colour": 5.6, "recall": 6.2}
# Beat around the Space press.
PRESS_LEAD, PRESS_TAIL = 1.1, 1.4

ALOUD = "aecho=0.86:0.9:22:0.10"


def run(cmd):
    r = subprocess.run(cmd, shell=True, capture_output=True, text=True)
    if r.returncode != 0:
        print(r.stderr[-1200:]); sys.exit("FAILED")


def build(slug, introduce):
    """introduce: which step cards to play. Q1 gets all; later ones get none."""
    segs = TL[slug]["segments"]
    src = os.path.join(AUD, f"{slug}.mp3")
    recall = os.path.join(GEN, f"{slug}.recall.wav")

    pieces, beats, t = [], [], 0.0

    def silence(dur, kind, **extra):
        nonlocal t
        p = os.path.join(TMP, f"{slug}_{len(pieces):03d}.wav")
        run(f'ffmpeg -y -v error -f lavfi -i anullsrc=r=48000:cl=mono '
            f'-t {dur:.3f} "{p}"')
        pieces.append(p)
        beats.append({"kind": kind, "start": round(t, 3), "end": round(t + dur, 3), **extra})
        t += dur

    def take(a, b, kind, **extra):
        nonlocal t
        p = os.path.join(TMP, f"{slug}_{len(pieces):03d}.wav")
        run(f'ffmpeg -y -v error -ss {a:.3f} -to {b:.3f} -i "{src}" '
            f'-af "{ALOUD}" -ar 48000 -ac 1 "{p}"')
        pieces.append(p)
        # srcStart maps this beat back onto the ORIGINAL recording, so caption
        # cues (written against his real run) still resolve after teaching beats
        # have been cut into the timeline.
        beats.append({"kind": kind, "start": round(t, 3), "end": round(t + (b - a), 3),
                      "srcStart": round(a, 3), **extra})
        t += b - a

    for i, s in enumerate(segs):
        gi, ns = s["gi"], s["chunkNs"]

        if i == 0 and "read" in introduce:
            silence(HOLD["read"], "card", step="read")
        # Step 1. Silent, and exactly as long as he really took.
        silence(s["readEnd"] - s["readStart"], "read", gi=gi, chunkNs=ns)

        if i == 0 and "tell" in introduce:
            silence(HOLD["tell"], "card", step="tell")
        # The release, then everything he says out loud, in one take.
        if s["saidStart"] > s["readEnd"]:
            take(s["readEnd"], s["saidStart"], "release", gi=gi, chunkNs=ns)

        if i == 0 and "colour" in introduce:
            silence(HOLD["colour"], "card", step="colour")
        take(s["saidStart"], s["workEnd"], "speak", gi=gi, chunkNs=ns)

    # The Recall: introduced, then the press, then he gives it back.
    if "recall" in introduce:
        silence(HOLD["recall"], "card", step="recall")
    silence(PRESS_LEAD, "press", phase="before")
    rdur = float(subprocess.run(
        f'ffprobe -v error -show_entries format=duration -of csv=p=0 "{recall}"',
        shell=True, capture_output=True, text=True).stdout.strip())
    p = os.path.join(TMP, f"{slug}_{len(pieces):03d}.wav")
    run(f'ffmpeg -y -v error -i "{recall}" -af "{ALOUD}" -ar 48000 -ac 1 "{p}"')
    pieces.append(p)
    beats.append({"kind": "recall", "start": round(t, 3), "end": round(t + rdur, 3)})
    t += rdur
    silence(PRESS_TAIL, "settle")

    lst = os.path.join(TMP, f"{slug}.txt")
    with io.open(lst, "w", encoding="utf-8") as fh:
        for p in pieces:
            fh.write(f"file '{os.path.basename(p)}'\n")
    voice = os.path.join(TMP, f"{slug}_voice.wav")
    run(f'ffmpeg -y -v error -f concat -safe 0 -i "{lst}" -c copy "{voice}"')

    # The bed, gated over the silent reading only, so it carries those stretches.
    gate = []
    for b in beats:
        if b["kind"] == "read" and b["end"] - b["start"] > 0.6:
            gate.append(f"between(t,{b['start']:.3f},{b['end']:.3f})*"
                        f"min(1,min((t-{b['start']:.3f})/0.55,({b['end']:.3f}-t)/0.5))")
    env = "+".join(gate) if gate else "0"
    bed = os.path.join(TMP, f"{slug}_bed.wav")
    run(f'ffmpeg -y -v error -f lavfi -i "sine=frequency=98:duration={t:.3f}:sample_rate=48000" '
        f'-f lavfi -i "sine=frequency=147:duration={t:.3f}:sample_rate=48000" '
        f'-filter_complex "[0:a]volume=0.075[a];[1:a]volume=0.030[b];[a][b]amix=inputs=2:normalize=0,'
        f'lowpass=f=420,volume=\'{env}\':eval=frame[o]" -map "[o]" -ar 48000 -ac 1 "{bed}"')

    out = os.path.join(AUD, f"{slug}.instr.mp3")
    run(f'ffmpeg -y -v error -i "{voice}" -i "{bed}" '
        f'-filter_complex "[0:a][1:a]amix=inputs=2:normalize=0:duration=first[o]" '
        f'-map "[o]" -codec:a libmp3lame -b:a 192k "{out}"')

    script = {"slug": slug, "duration": round(t, 3), "beats": beats}
    json.dump(script, io.open(os.path.join(BASE, f"public/content/{slug}.script.json"),
                              "w", encoding="utf-8"), indent=1)

    got = float(subprocess.run(
        f'ffprobe -v error -show_entries format=duration -of csv=p=0 "{out}"',
        shell=True, capture_output=True, text=True).stdout.strip())
    kinds = {}
    for b in beats:
        kinds[b["kind"]] = kinds.get(b["kind"], 0) + 1
    print(f"{slug}: {len(beats)} beats {kinds}")
    print(f"   script {t:.2f}s | audio {got:.2f}s | "
          f"{'OK' if abs(got - t) < 0.15 else '*** DRIFT ***'} | "
          f"was {TL[slug]['duration']:.2f}s, +{t - TL[slug]['duration']:.1f}s of teaching")


# Q1 introduces every step. Q2 and Q3 are already understood, so they only
# introduce nothing at all: the labels alone carry them (R5-Q1).
build("PT105_S2_Q1", {"read", "tell", "colour", "recall"})
for s in ("PT105_S2_Q2", "PT105_S2_Q3"):
    build(s, set())

/** Named while it is happening, once the step has been introduced. */
export const StepLabel: React.FC<{ step: StepKey; enter: number }> = ({ step, enter }) => {
  const s = STEPS[step];
  return (
    <Plate3D x={205} y={118} w={330} tiltX={5} tiltY={7} rgb="150,170,255" enter={enter}>
      {/* Backed in dark, because this label sits over the white paper card and

      <div

          {s.name}
        </span>
      </div>
    </Plate3D>
  );
};

/** The full stop. Picture holds, his audio is silent, this is all there is. */
export const StepCard: React.FC<{ step: StepKey; k: number }> = ({ step, k }) => {
  const s = STEPS[step];
  const enter = interpolate(k, [0, 0.16], [0, 1], { extrapolateRight: "clamp", easing: Easing.out(Easing.cubic) });
  const leave = interpolate(k, [0.88, 1], [1, 0], { extrapolateLeft: "clamp", easing: Easing.in(Easing.cubic) });
  const on = Math.min(enter, leave);
  return (
    <>
      {/* The room quietens so the instruction is unmistakably not part of it. */}
      <div
        style={{
          position: "absolute", inset: 0, zIndex: 3,
          background: "rgba(6,7,14,0.62)", opacity: on,
        }}
      />
      <div style={{ position: "absolute", inset: 0, zIndex: 5, display: "flex",
                    alignItems: "center", justifyContent: "center" }}>
        <Plate3D x={0} y={0} w={980} tiltX={3} tiltY={-4} rgb="150,170,255" enter={on} flow>
          <div style={{ padding: "14px 16px" }}>
            <div style={{ display: "flex", alignItems: "baseline", gap: 15, marginBottom: 20 }}>

                {s.name}
              </span>
            </div>
            <div style={{ fontSize: 34, lineHeight: 1.45, color: "rgba(255,255,255,0.92)" }}>
              {s.says}

    </>
  );
};
=== Float.tsx ===
import React from "react";
import { interpolate, spring, Easing } from "remotion";
import { SEC } from "../rrc/tokens";

/*
 * Things that hang in the room.
 *
 * Technique lifted from the HyperFrames kit's t2-lt-nameplate3d / t2-lb-bubble /
 * t2-lb-checkmark cards: a perspective stage, a card leaning in space with real
 * side depth, and a LOCAL bloom behind each card rather than one global glow.
 * The local bloom is what makes a card read as lit by the room instead of
 * pasted onto the frame.
 *
 * These deliberately do not use the app's palette. They must never be mistaken
 * for part of the drill, and here that is guaranteed by geometry: nothing in the
 * real drill floats in your room.
 */

const GLASS =
  "linear-gradient(135deg, rgba(255,255,255,.10) 0%, rgba(255,255,255,.035) 38%, " +
  "rgba(255,255,255,.014) 66%, rgba(255,255,255,.075) 100%)";

const Bloom: React.FC<{ w: number; h: number; rgb: string; a?: number }> = ({ w, h, rgb, a = 0.3 }) => (
  <div
    style={{
      position: "absolute", left: -w * 0.18, top: -h * 0.22,
      width: w * 1.36, height: h * 1.44, borderRadius: "50%",
      background: `radial-gradient(ellipse at 42% 78%, rgba(${rgb},${a}) 0%, rgba(${rgb},${a * 0.33}) 48%, transparent 74%)`,
      filter: "blur(38px)", pointerEvents: "none", zIndex: 0,
    }}
  />
);

/** A card leaning in the room's space. Children sit on its face. */
export const Plate3D: React.FC<{
  x: number; y: number; w: number;
  tiltX?: number; tiltY?: number;
  rgb?: string; enter: number;           // 0..1
  /* Cards that hang in the ROOM are absolutely placed in room space. The
     instruction cards are not objects in the room, they are the film stopping
     to talk, so they lay out in normal flow and let a flex parent centre them. */
  flow?: boolean;
  children: React.ReactNode;
}> = ({ x, y, w, tiltX = 7, tiltY = -9, rgb = "150,170,255", enter, flow = false, children }) => {
  const lift = interpolate(enter, [0, 1], [26, 0], { easing: Easing.out(Easing.cubic) });
  const scale = interpolate(enter, [0, 1], [0.9, 1], { easing: Easing.out(Easing.cubic) });
  return (
    <div style={flow
      ? { position: "relative", perspective: 1200, perspectiveOrigin: "50% 50%", zIndex: 4 }
      : { position: "absolute", left: x, top: y, perspective: 900, perspectiveOrigin: "34% 58%", zIndex: 4 }}>
      <div
        style={{
          position: "relative", width: w, transformStyle: "preserve-3d",
          transform: `rotateX(${tiltX}deg) rotateY(${tiltY}deg) translateY(${lift.toFixed(2)}px) scale(${scale.toFixed(3)})`,
          opacity: interpolate(enter, [0, 0.35], [0, 1], { extrapolateRight: "clamp" }),
        }}
      >
        <Bloom w={w} h={130} rgb={rgb} />
        {/* extruded side depth, so the card has real thickness */}
        <div
          style={{
            position: "absolute", inset: 0, borderRadius: 16, zIndex: 1,
            transform: "translateZ(-13px)",
            background: `rgba(${rgb},0.30)`,
            filter: "blur(1px)",
          }}
        />
        <div
          style={{
            position: "relative", zIndex: 2, borderRadius: 16,
            background: GLASS,
            backdropFilter: "blur(14px) saturate(1.12)",
            WebkitBackdropFilter: "blur(14px) saturate(1.12)",
            border: "1px solid rgba(255,255,255,0.17)",
            boxShadow:
              "inset 0 1px 0 rgba(255,255,255,0.22), 0 26px 60px rgba(0,0,0,0.46)",
            padding: "20px 24px",
            fontFamily: SEC.sans, color: "#fff",
          }}
        >
          {children}
        </div>
      </div>
    </div>
  );
};

/** What he is saying, arriving as he says it. */
export const SpeechBubble: React.FC<{ text: string; enter: number; x: number; y: number }> = ({
  text, enter, x, y,
}) => (
  <Plate3D x={x} y={y} w={560} tiltX={6} tiltY={-11} rgb="150,170,255" enter={enter}>
    <div style={{ fontSize: 33, lineHeight: 1.36, fontWeight: 400, letterSpacing: -0.2 }}>{text}</div>
    {/* pointer tail, back toward him */}
    <div
      style={{
        position: "absolute", left: 44, bottom: -13, width: 30, height: 30,
        background: "rgba(255,255,255,0.075)",
        borderRight: "1px solid rgba(255,255,255,0.17)",
        borderBottom: "1px solid rgba(255,255,255,0.17)",
        transform: "rotate(45deg)", borderRadius: 4,
      }}
    />
  </Plate3D>
);

/** A tick that draws itself on, next to the thing it confirms. */
export const CheckLine: React.FC<{ text: string; enter: number }> = ({ text, enter }) => {
  const draw = interpolate(enter, [0, 0.55], [1, 0], { extrapolateRight: "clamp", easing: Easing.out(Easing.cubic) });
  return (
    <div style={{ display: "flex", alignItems: "flex-start", gap: 13, opacity: interpolate(enter, [0, 0.2], [0, 1], { extrapolateRight: "clamp" }) }}>
      <svg width={26} height={26} viewBox="0 0 36 36" fill="none" style={{ flex: "0 0 auto", marginTop: 3 }}>
        <defs>
          <filter id="ckGlow" x="-40%" y="-40%" width="180%" height="180%">
            <feGaussianBlur stdDeviation="2.4" result="b" />
            <feMerge><feMergeNode in="b" /><feMergeNode in="SourceGraphic" /></feMerge>
          </filter>
        </defs>
        <path
          d="M6 19 L14.5 27 L30 9"
          stroke="#7ee0b0" strokeWidth={4.2} strokeLinecap="round" strokeLinejoin="round"
          filter="url(#ckGlow)"
          pathLength={1} strokeDasharray={1} strokeDashoffset={draw}
        />
      </svg>
      <span style={{ fontSize: 26, lineHeight: 1.34, color: "rgba(255,255,255,0.94)" }}>{text}</span>
    </div>
  );
};

/**
 * Springs an entry in, so arrivals feel physical rather than timed.
 * Deliberately a plain function, not a hook: these are called inside maps and
 * conditionals, where a hook would be illegal.
 */
export function enterAt(framesSince: number, fps: number, stiffness = 108): number {
  if (framesSince < 0) return 0;
  return spring({ frame: framesSince, fps, config: { damping: 17, stiffness, mass: 0.7 } });
}

Confirmed the glitch, and it's structural:

| beat | label shown | Q1 durations |

"Tell it back" springs in and vanishes in under half a second — that's the flicker you saw. And the 34-second colouring stretch is the one that goes too far. Both live in the same wrong assumption: that the retell is the pause between reading and speaking. It isn't. The retell is the *first part of what he says*, and the colouring is the reaction that follows it.

## assistant

Boundary is clean in all seven segments. His retell always opens "Okay. So…" and the colouring starts at a reaction marker ("It's like", "This is like", "which sure", "Well, what if"). And I can see the two stretches that go too far:

---
name: cut-mistakes
description: "Agent 2 of the video editing pipeline. Finds and removes spoken mistakes — stutters, repeated words, false starts, and retakes (re-recorded lines) — from a talking-head recording. Use after cut-silences, when asked to cut mistakes, remove stutters / repeats / filler restarts, clean up flubs, or keep the best take. Works review-gated: it proposes every cut with context and a reason for approval, then renders only the approved cuts via ffmpeg. Requires a word-level transcript."
---

# Cut Mistakes & Repeats (Pipeline Agent 2)

Second step of the automated edit, run after **cut-silences**. It removes the things a human editor cuts on a second pass: stutters ("the the", "I- I-"), immediately repeated words, false starts (an abandoned phrase that restarts), and retakes (a line re-recorded — keep the clean take, drop the botched one).

Deciding what counts as a mistake is **judgment, not a formula** — emphatic repetition ("never, never") and rhetorical doubling ("the first piece is, is this…") look identical to a stutter mechanically. So this agent is **review-gated by default**: it surfaces candidates with full context; the agent (and the user) decide; only approved cuts are rendered.

## When to use

- "cut the mistakes", "remove stutters / repeats", "clean up the flubs", "keep the best take", "cut false starts"
- As the second stage of the master edit workflow, on cut-silences' output.

## The three-part flow

### 1. Find candidates (mechanical)

```bash
node .claude/skills/cut-mistakes/scripts/find-cut-candidates.mjs \
  <silence-transcript.json> --out-dir video-projects/<slug>/assets
```

Input is normally Agent 1's `<stem>.silence-transcript.json`, so cuts land on the already-silenced timeline. Writes:

- `<stem>.cut-candidates.json` — structured candidates (type, confidence, proposed `cut` range, `removes`/`keeps` text, context, recommendation)
- `<stem>.cut-candidates.md` — readable proposal

Candidate types: `stutter` (immediate word repeat), `retake` (duplicate/near-duplicate segment via Jaccard similarity), `false_start` (short abandoned phrase that restarts).

### 2. Review (the gate — REQUIRED)

Read each candidate **in context** and decide keep-or-cut. The mechanical detector cannot tell intentional emphasis/rhetoric from a real flub, so do not trust `recommend` blindly — read `removes` + `context`. Common false positives: emphatic repetition ("it's never, never hands-off"), copula-then-question ("the piece is, is this…"), listing.

Present the candidates to the user with a recommendation per item; collect approvals. Write the approved cuts to a file:

```json
{ "cuts": [ { "start": 100.93, "end": 101.06, "reason": "I- false start" }, ... ] }
```

(`start`/`end` in seconds on the input transcript/video timeline. You can widen a candidate's range — e.g. for a retake, cut from the botched take's start to the clean restart.)

### 3. Apply approved cuts

```bash
node .claude/skills/cut-mistakes/scripts/apply-cuts.mjs \
  <silence-transcript.json> --cuts approved-cuts.json \
  --video video-projects/<slug>/assets/edited-silenced.mp4 \
  --output video-projects/<slug>/assets/edited-clean.mp4 --apply
```

Writes the EDL, a re-timed `<stem>.mistakes-transcript.json` (feeds the motion-graphics agent + `validate-beat-sync.mjs`), a decisions log, and — with `--apply` — the cut video (ffmpeg trim+concat, A/V in sync). Drop `--apply` for a dry run.

## Reviewing the result

Use the shared review tool to eyeball the cuts and spot-check boundaries:

```bash

apply-cuts.mjs
find-cut-candidates.mjs
########## cut-silences
---
name: cut-silences
description: Agent 1 of the video editing pipeline. Removes silences and dead air from a talking-head recording. Use when asked to cut silences, trim pauses, remove dead air / gaps, or tighten the pacing of a raw video, given a word-level transcript. Produces an edit list (EDL), a re-timed transcript for downstream agents, and optionally the cut video via ffmpeg. Does NOT cut mistakes, repeats, or false starts — that is the cut-mistakes agent.
---

# Cut Silences (Pipeline Agent 1)

First step of the automated edit. Takes a raw recording + its word-level transcript and removes **only silence**: dead air before the first word and after the last word, plus inter-word pauses longer than a threshold (trimmed down to a natural breath, never a hard zero-gap). It leaves the speaker's words untouched — false starts, retakes, and stutters are the **cut-mistakes** agent's job (Agent 2).

This agent is deterministic and transcript-driven, so its output (`*.silence-transcript.json`) feeds cleanly into the next agents and the beat-sync validator.

## When to use

- "cut the silences", "trim the pauses", "remove dead air", "tighten the pacing"
- As the first stage of the master edit workflow, right after transcription.

## Prerequisites

A word-level transcript JSON. Either the ElevenLabs Scribe shape
(`{ words: [{ te

... [5523 characters truncated] ...

graphic every few seconds
makes the viewer restart comprehension every few seconds, and no individual
graphic is at fault, which is why the feedback comes back as the vague
"confusing" or "too much going on" rather than as a specific defect.

For a neutral practice beat sheet, read `docs/STORYTELLING-WORKBOOK.md` at the
repository root. The reusable design system is in this skill's
`reference/design-system.md`; the worked open-loop geometry is in
`reference/patterns/wall-of-slots/`. Private source videos are not required.

This is not `hyperframes-video-beats`. That skill places overlay cards on top of
a talking head. This one designs the thing the video is *about*.

## 1 · The four principles

### One persistent world

The video lives in a single space, and it never resets. Everything the viewer has
already seen still exists where they left it, and new material is added to it
rather than replacing it. An element, once drawn, is never redrawn, moved, or
relabelled.

Everything else in this skill depends on that. The camera has nowhere to go if
the space keeps changing, a spotlight means nothing if there is nothing else on
screen, and an open loop cannot stay open in a world that resets.

The world also has to be worth staying in, so decide the spatial grammar before
building anything and hold it for the whole runtime. In the reference build:
left to right is toward the outcome, up is toward a human and is the only warm
thing on screen, down is into a system of record. A viewer who learns that in the
first two minutes reads the rest of the video for free.

Break the grammar exactly once, deliberately, for meaning. The reference build's
only right-to-left move in ten minutes is a resolved ticket reversing back into
the queue on the words "reopen rate". It lands without a word because direction
had meant something for the previous three minutes.

### The camera is the edit

Cutting to a new graphic costs the viewer their bearings every time. Moving
through a space that stays put costs them nothing. A push-in then reads as "look
closer at the part of the thing you already understand", and the pull-back is
where the work pays off, because everything built so far is still there.

Work in three altitudes and move between adjacent ones:

| Altitude | Shows | Used for |
| --- | --- | --- |
| World | the whole space | orienting, payoffs, "here is where we are" |

########## hyperframes-video-beats
---
name: hyperframes-video-beats
description: Plan, write, time, style, and validate motion-graphic beats for long-form talking-head HyperFrames videos. Use when Codex is asked to add or improve visual beats, glassmorphism lower thirds, crumpled-paper full-screen takeovers, transcript-synced callouts, pacing coverage, YouTube retention graphics, or render-ready overlays in an existing HyperFrames video project.
---

# HyperFrames Video Beats

Use this skill to turn a talking-head video plus transcript into a paced set of motion graphics that support the narration without covering the speaker unnecessarily.

## Core Goal

Keep the viewer oriented and engaged. Every beat must answer one of these:

- What should the viewer remember?
- What concept needs visual structure?
- What stat, contrast, or pivot deserves emphasis?
- Where is the pacing getting quiet enough that the viewer may drift?

Do not add graphics just because a gap exists. Add graphics because the narration has a topic, transition, proof point, or mental model that benefits from visual support.

## First Pass

1. Read project instructions (`AGENTS.md`, local skill docs, and project-specific notes).
2. Inspect the root composition, current beat schedule, shared CSS, and transcript.
3. Build a coverage map from the root `data-start` / `data-duration` entries or the master `SHOW` array.
4. Extract transcript chunks for any requested region and any uncovered gaps.
5. Identify topic spans by narration, not by arbitrary timestamps.
6. Decide whether each topic needs a full-screen paper beat, a lower-third glass card, or no new beat.

Use fast local commands. Prefer `rg`, `Select-String`, and small Node/PowerShell transcript scripts.

## Beat Type Decision

### Use Full-Screen Crumpled Paper

Use a paper takeover when the narration reaches a major thesis, list, section marker, quote, or stat that should briefly become the whole frame.

Good uses:

- Hook payoff or "in this video" overview
- Major section transition
- Big stat or study result
- Memorable thesis line
- Short quote or mantra
- Contrast that needs the audience to stop and look

Rules:

- Keep it short, usually 4-12 seconds.
- Use sparse black text.
- Let only true stat numbers or one extremely important word use accent color.
- Do not cover long explanations with paper takeovers.
- Do not make full-screen text playful, huge, or over-bold by default.

### Use Glassmorphism Lower Third

Use a glass lower-third when the speaker should remain visible and the graphic should support the current idea.

That's the authoritative source, and it changes several things. From the app's own briefing:

**The real step labels** are `Read it.` / `Tell it back.` / `Colour it.` / `The Recall.` — so two of my four cards are wrong ("Reading" and "Colouring In" are my inventions). It also settles the earlier question: the app says **Tell it back**, not Rephrase.

**Space is pressed twice**, not once. First press takes the text away and asks for the recall; second press loads the next stimulus. My film only presses once.

And the finding that matters most — this is a design principle the film currently contradicts:

> "While you are working the stimulus, nothing on the screen hides. The whole thing stays readable… the only thing stopping you rereading a line while you tell it back is you, looking away from it." … "A screen that hides the text for you is a crutch that cannot come with you."

My film blurs every inactive chunk to 2.6px and blurs *everything* to 3.1px while he speaks. So it teaches the opposite of the drill: it shows the app hiding text for you. That's why "review before you recreate it" has nowhere to happen — I blurred the page the student is supposed to be able to re-read.

Let me confirm the runner's actual phases before I grill you on it.

# Grill Me

Relentlessly interview the user about every aspect of the topic until you reach shared understanding. Walk down each branch of the decision tree, resolving dependencies one by one. The real goal is to **extract what's in their head into a durable, organized markdown file** so nothing is lost as context fills up.

## The capture file is the whole point

Long interviews fill up context. If you hold answers only in your head, you will eventually misremember, conflate, or drop something. So you **checkpoint to disk after every single answer**. The file, not your context, is the source of truth. Never make the user ask you to save progress.

## Existing context and scope

Read the applicable `AGENTS.md` and `CLAUDE.md`, then only the context pages and prior captures relevant to the topic. Starter-kit context lives in `context/`, but follow an established project's declared routes instead of creating a duplicate knowledge store. Reuse facts already supplied; ask about gaps, changes, decisions, and tradeoffs. Installing the skill does not start an interview.

## Setup (do this BEFORE the first question)

1. **Create the capture file** at `brainstorms/{YYYY-MM-DD}-{topic-slug}.md` (create the `brainstorms/` folder if it doesn't exist). Every brainstorm capture lives here. One predictable home, regardless of topic. Do NOT scatter captures into project folders. If a session later produces a polished deliverable (a plan, a map, a spec), that artifact can move into the relevant `projects/` folder, but the raw capture always stays in `brainstorms/`.
   - Get today's actual date using the available clock or shell (`Get-Date -Format yyyy-MM-dd` in PowerShell, `date +%F` in Bash).
   - Never overwrite an existing capture. For a new session with the same date/topic, add a time or unique suffix and create exclusively. Resume an existing capture only when the user requests or clearly refers to that session; read it first and continue its question numbering.
   - If no topic is supplied or inferable, create an `untitled-discovery` capture with goal pending, then ask what to explore. Record the chosen topic and goal in that same file.
2. **Create the file immediately** with a header: title, date, the goal of the session, and an empty "Open flags" section.
3. **Tell the user where you're saving**, in one line. Then ask Q1.

## The checkpoint rule (non-negotiable)

After EVERY user answer, BEFORE you ask the next question:
- Append a structured entry to the capture file: the question topic, the key facts and decisions from their answer (in their words where the wording matters), and any flags (things they couldn't answer plus who should).
- Update the running summary when a later answer changes it. Preserve the original Q&A entry, mark it superseded, and link to the correcting answer so the reasoning remains traceable.
- Read back the saved entry before continuing. If saving fails, explain the failure and keep the answer visible in chat; do not claim it was saved or continue collecting answers without a working checkpoint.
- Only then ask the next question.

Never batch multiple answers into one write. Checkpoint one answer at a time. The point is that if context is lost at any moment, the file already holds everything said so far.

## Interview method

- Ask **one question at a time**. For decisions, provide a suggested answer grounded in context and label it as a suggestion. For personal or business facts, ask neutrally; do not lead the user with invented facts or save your inference as their answer.
- Keep user-confirmed facts, tentative ideas, assistant suggestions, and unresolved questions distinguishable in the capture.
- Resolve dependencies in order: settle the upstream decision before the ones that depend on it.
- If a question can be answered by **exploring the codebase or reading a file/doc**, do that instead of asking. If the user hands you a doc (e.g. a Google Doc), read it and only surface what's net-new.
- When the user **can't answer** something, capture it as a flag with the right owner and move on. Don't stall.
- Keep going until the user says you're done, needs a pause, or you've covered the useful branches. Respect an explicit stop immediately; do not add a new question. Offer a completeness backstop near the end ("anything we haven't touched?").

## Capture file structure

```
# {Topic}: Brainstorm / Discovery Notes
Date: {date} · Goal: {one line}
Status: in progress / paused / complete
Context sources: {relevant existing pages, if any}

## Summary / key decisions
(running synthesis, updated as you go)

## Q&A log
### Q1 - {topic}
- Asked: {question}
- Captured: {user-confirmed facts, decisions, in their words where it matters}
- Tentative / suggested: {unconfirmed ideas, clearly labeled}
- Flags: {open item -> owner}
...

## Open flags (pending input)
- {item} -> {who can answer}
```

## At the end or on pause
- Read the capture for contradictions or gaps. Mark unresolved conflicts explicitly; do not choose a business fact on the user's behalf. Update the session status and resume point.
- For sessions explicitly about building or updating AI OS context, merge user-confirmed durable facts and preferences into the appropriate existing context page, with a dated link back to the capture. Preserve unrelated content. Record confirmed meaningful decisions in the existing decision log without duplicating entries. Unknowns, brainstorm ideas, and assistant suggestions stay labeled in the capture until confirmed.
- For a plan/design interview without a request to update canonical context, keep the raw capture as the output. Suggest `/link` for routing a useful result, or `/level-up` for turning a selected opportunity into one improvement. Do not run either automatically.
- Ensure the operating manual or an existing index routes to `brainstorms/` when capture routing is absent, using the project's conventions and preserving shared manual parity. Individual captures do not each need a root-manual entry. Raw captures are dated interview evidence, not automatically current business truth.
- Give a short recap with a clickable capture path, context pages actually updated, remaining flags, and the next step or resume point.

## Boundaries and verification

- Local interview capture and requested context updates are the scope. No global-memory edits, publication, messages, or external-system changes. Do not request or save credentials; use references or redactions for secrets.
- The starter kit ignores `brainstorms/` in Git by default. This does not remove captures already tracked in an existing repository.
- Validate a fresh session, same-day name collision, resume, a correcting answer, a stop request, and a failed write. Verify that only confirmed facts reach canonical context and that earlier answers remain traceable.

**R6-Q2 — the review beat.** I want to be precise here because there's a wrinkle.

The app never says "review it" as a step. What it says is "press space **when you have got all of it**", and it keeps the whole page readable so you *can* look back. So reviewing is permitted by design, not prescribed. Your phrase was "what I was meant to do" — which I read as: your recording didn't do it, but the demonstration should show the intended method anyway.

One tension worth naming, then I'll take your call: if the film shows him re-reading the whole stimulus immediately before the recall, a student could copy that and recall from a fresh re-read rather than from what they retained — which is the thing the drill is measuring. There's a difference between "look over it once and check you've got it" and "read it again".

Also: his real audio has no such beat, so this has to be a **silent held beat** — picture only, whatever we decide.

## assistant

**R6-Q3 — where "Tell it back" ends and "Colour it" begins.** This is the glitch's real cause, and it's a teaching judgment, so it's yours.

Right now the film labels the 0.4s breath between reading and speaking as "Tell it back", and the entire 17–34s of speech as "Colour it". Both wrong. The retell is the *first part of what he says*; the colour is the reaction that follows it. Splitting them correctly fixes the flicker and gives step 3 an honest duration.

From his own transcript, the pivot is visible every time:

> **Tell it back** — "Okay. So these French divers, they're diving off the coast of the Mediterranean Sea, and they recently found this large cave."
> **Colour it** — "It's like I'm picturing, an underwater cave, where maybe it used to be above water…"

> **Tell it back** — "Okay. So this manager is saying saving money is important, so therefore, we should install the network during the day."
> **Colour it** — "Well, what if the higher installation charges are not as much of a cost…"

So the rule I'd derive: **the retell ends at the first thing that isn't in the text.** The moment he adds a picture, a doubt, a comparison, or a "what if", he's colouring. One case I can't call for you — Q1 chunk 3, "…totally filled with water, like floor to ceiling. **You cannot breathe. You need a scuba tank.**" That reads to me like colour, not retell, but it's your drill.

**R6-Q9 — the read-along light.** Last one, then I build.

You asked for the text to light up as he reads so it's clear where the eye is. There's a rule from Round 1 this bumps into: *nothing marks the text*, because anything drawn on the page can be mistaken for an app feature the student won't have. That rule is why I used blur in the first place.

But killing the blur changes the calculus. Blur worked by *removing* legibility, which is the thing your briefing refuses. A light works by *adding* emphasis while everything stays fully readable — which is compatible with "nothing hides". So it can be done honestly; it just has to read as light falling on a page, not as a highlighter.

The pace comes free: I know his real read duration and the word count, so the light moves at the speed he actually read.

from chatterbox.tts import ChatterboxTTS

BASE = r"C:/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os/projects/video/recall"
REF = os.path.join(BASE, "voice-ref.wav")
OUT = os.path.join(BASE, "generated")
os.makedirs(OUT, exist_ok=True)

scripts = json.load(open(os.path.join(BASE, "scripts.json"), encoding="utf-8"))

def sentences(text):
    parts = re.split(r"(?<=[.?!])\s+", text.strip())
    return [p.strip() for p in parts if p.strip()]

print("loading model...", flush=True)
t0 = time.time()
model = ChatterboxTTS.from_pretrained(device="cpu")
print(f"model loaded in {time.time()-t0:.1f}s  sr={model.sr}", flush=True)

for slug in ["PT105_S2_Q1", "PT105_S2_Q2", "PT105_S2_Q3"]:
    text = scripts[slug]
    sents = sentences(text)
    print(f"\n=== {slug}: {len(sents)} sentences, {len(text.split())} words ===", flush=True)
    chunks, timing, cursor = [], [], 0.0
    for i, s in enumerate(sents):
        t1 = time.time()
        wav = model.generate(s, audio_prompt_path=REF)
        dur = wav.shape[-1] / model.sr
        chunks.append(wav)
        timing.append({"index": i, "text": s, "start": round(cursor, 3),
                       "end": round(cursor + dur, 3), "duration": round(dur, 3)})
        cursor += dur
        print(f"  [{i+1}/{len(sents)}] {dur:5.2f}s audio in {time.time()-t1:5.1f}s  | {s[:60]}", flush=True)

    full = torch.cat(chunks, dim=-1)
    wav_path = os.path.join(OUT, f"{slug}.recall.wav")
    ta.save(wav_path, full, model.sr)
    json.dump({"slug": slug, "sample_rate": model.sr,
               "total_duration": round(cursor, 3), "sentences": timing},
              open(os.path.join(OUT, f"{slug}.recall.timing.json"), "w", encoding="utf-8"),
              indent=2)
    print(f"WROTE {wav_path}  {cursor:.1f}s total", flush=True)

print("\nALL DONE", flush=True)

Same words, same meaning, cleaner seams.

That is the difference between hearing \"every chunk\n * combined\" and seeing it.\n *\n * Why this does not break the Round 1 rule that annotations must never read as\n * app UI: the rule is satisfied by GEOMETRY, not styling. Nothing in the real drill floats in\n * your room.\n */\n\nexport type RecallSentence = { index: number; text: string; start: number; end: number };\n\n/** Lines shorter than this are beats, not content, and never get their own card. */\nconst MIN_WORDS = 3;\n\nexport const RecallStack: React.FC<{\n  sentences: RecallSentence[];\n  /** Seconds since the recall beat began. */\n  t: number;\n  fps: number;\n}> = ({ sentences, t, fps }) => {\n  const lines = sentences.filter((s) => s.text.trim().split(/\\s+/).length >= MIN_WORDS);\n  const shown = lines.filter((s) => t >= s.start);\n  if (shown.length === 0) return null;\n\n  /*\n   * Everything said so far stays on screen, so the column has to fit the whole\n   * argument by the end. Math.ceil(s.text.length / 46) * 0.5, 0);\n  const fit = Math.min(1, 4.6 / budget);\n  const size = Math.max(19, Math.round(30 * fit));\n\n  return (\n    <div\n      style={{\n        position: \"absolute\", left: 232, top: 150, width: 1180,\n        perspective: 1150, perspectiveOrigin: \"42% 46%\", zIndex: 6,\n      }}\n    >\n      <div\n        style={{\n          transformStyle: \"preserve-3d\",\n          transform: \"rotateX(4deg) rotateY(-7deg)\",\n          display: \"flex\", flexDirection: \"column\", gap: Math.round(11 * fit),\n        }}\n      >\n        {shown.map((s) => {\n          const since = Math.round((t - s.start) * fps);\n          const e = enterAt(since, fps, 96);\n          /* Older lines settle back in Z so the newest reads as the live one,\n             while everything already said stays fully legible. */\n          const age = shown.length - 1 - shown.indexOf(s);\n          const back = Math.min(age, 5) * 5;\n          const dim = interpolate(Math.min(age, 5), [0, 5], [1, 0.72]);\n          return (\n            <div\n              key={s.index}\n              style={{\n                transform:\n                  `translateZ(${(-back).toFixed(1)}px) ` +\n                  `translateX(${interpolate(e, [0, 1], [34, 0]).toFixed(1)}px)`,\n                opacity: interpolate(e, [0, 0.4], [0, dim], { extrapolateRight: \"clamp\" }),\n                transformOrigin: \"0% 50%\",\n              }}\n            >\n              <div\n                style={{\n                  display: \"inline-block\",\n                  background: \"rgba(9,11,26,0.80)\",\n                  borderLeft: \"3px solid rgba(169,188,255,0.85)\",\n                  borderRadius: \"4px 10px 10px 4px\",\n                  padding: `${Math.round(9 * fit)}px ${Math.round(17 * fit)}px`,\n                  fontFamily: SEC.sans,\n                  fontSize: size,\n                  lineHeight: 1.34,\n                  letterSpacing: -0.2,\n                  color: \"#fff\",\n                  boxShadow: \"0 18px 40px rgba(0,0,0,0.42)\",\n                }}\n              >\n                {s.text}\n              </div>\n            </div>\n          );\n        })}\n      </div>\n    </div>\n  );\n};\n\n/**\n * The count, so the accumulation has a number on it.\n *\n * Sits under the stack and only appears once more than one line is up, because\n * \"1 of 4\" next to a single line states the obvious.\n */\nexport const RecallProgress: React.FC<{ done: number; total: number; enter: number }> = ({\n  done, total, enter,\n}) => {\n  if (done < 2) return null;\n  return (\n    <div\n      style={{\n        position: \"absolute\", left: 232, top: 838, zIndex: 6,\n        opacity: interpolate(enter, [0, 1], [0, 0.92], { extrapolateRight: \"clamp\" }),\n        fontFamily: SEC.sans, fontSize: 21, letterSpacing: 1.6,\n        color: \"#a9bcff\",\n        background: \"rgba(9,11,26,0.72)\", borderRadius: 7, padding: \"7px 13px\",\n      }}\n    >\n      {done} OF {total} BACK\n    </div>\n  );\n};\n\n/** Eases the whole stack away at the very end of the beat. */\nexport function stackLeave(k: number): number {\n  return interpolate(k, [0.94, 1], [1, 0], {\n    extrapolateLeft: \"clamp\", extrapolateRight: \"clamp\", easing: Easing.in(Easing.cubic),\n  });\n}\n"}

import React from "react";
import { SEC, TEACH } from "./tokens";

export type Chunk = { n: number; text: string; details: { n: number; text: string }[] };

/* The stimulus exactly as the drill renders it: every chunk in order, nothing
   marking one out from another. The teaching highlight is layered ON TOP and is
   the only thing here the real screen does not do. */
export const Stimulus: React.FC<{
  chunks: Chunk[];
  activeNs: number[];
  reading: boolean;
}> = ({ chunks, activeNs, reading }) => (
  <div
    style={{
      maxWidth: "52ch", color: SEC.ink, fontFamily: SEC.serif,
      fontSize: SEC.stimulusPx, fontWeight: 400, lineHeight: SEC.stimulusLh,
      fontOpticalSizing: "none", fontVariationSettings: '"opsz" 14',
    }}
  >
    {chunks.map((c) => {
      const on = activeNs.includes(c.n);
      return (
        <span
          key={c.n}
          style={{
            background: on ? TEACH.amberSoft : "transparent",
            boxShadow: on ? `inset 0 -2px 0 ${TEACH.amber}` : "none",
            borderRadius: 2,
            transition: "background 200ms",
          }}
        >
          {c.text}{" "}
        </span>
      );
    })}
  </div>
);

export const Captions: React.FC<{ text: string }> = ({ text }) =>

{"replace_all": false, "file_path": "C:\\Users\\Joshu\\dev\\ai-os\\projects\\video\\src\\pov\\quad.ts", "old_string": "/** The quad in frame pixels, for a frame of the given size (plate covers it). */\nexport function screenQuadPx(frameW: number, frameH: number): [Pt, Pt, Pt, Pt] {\n  // The plate is scaled to COVER the frame, then centred.\n  const scale = Math.max(frameW / PLATE_W, frameH / PLATE_H);\n  const drawnW = PLATE_W * scale, drawnH = PLATE_H * scale;\n  const offX = (frameW - drawnW) / 2, offY = (frameH - drawnH) / 2;\n  return SCREEN_QUAD.map(\n    ([nx, ny]) => [offX + nx * drawnW, offY + ny * drawnH] as Pt,\n  ) as [Pt, Pt, Pt, Pt];\n}", "new_string": "/** The quad in frame pixels, for a frame of the given size (plate covers it). */\nexport function screenQuadPx(frameW: number, frameH: number): [Pt, Pt, Pt, Pt] {\n  // The plate is scaled to COVER the frame, then centred.\n  const scale = Math.max(frameW / PLATE_W, frameH / PLATE_H);\n  const drawnW = PLATE_W * scale, drawnH = PLATE_H * scale;\n  const offX = (frameW - drawnW) / 2, offY = (frameH - drawnH) / 2;\n  return SCREEN_QUAD.map(\n    ([nx, ny]) => [offX + nx * drawnW, offY + ny * drawnH] as Pt,\n  ) as [Pt, Pt, Pt, Pt];\n}\n\n/**\n * How far the camera must translate to bring the monitor to frame centre.\n *\n * R6: he flagged the screen as off centre, and the frame grabs confirm it. The\n * cause is in the plate, not the camera: the detected quad spans x 0.071..0.616,\n * so the monitor's centroid sits at x = 0.34 of a plate that gets drawn edge to\n * edge. The camera never compensated, so the monitor has always sat a third of\n * the way across with dead wall to the right.\n *\n * The catch is that panning right pulls the plate's LEFT edge toward frame, and\n * there is no image beyond it. How far we can pan is therefore capped by zoom:\n *\n *     maxShift = (drawnW * zoom - frameW) / 2\n *\n * Fully centring needs zoom >= 1.45, at which the monitor stands 994px tall in a\n * 1080 frame and the room around it is gone. That trade is his to make, so this\n * takes a `pull` and CLAMPS rather than pretending the full move is available.\n * Whatever is left over after clamping is reported in `shortfall` so the caller\n * can be honest about it instead of silently under-delivering.\n */\nexport function centringShift(\n  frameW: number, frameH: number, zoom: number, pull = 1, headroom = 8,\n): { x: number; y: number; shortfall: number } {\n  const scale = Math.max(frameW / PLATE_W, frameH / PLATE_H);\n  const drawnW = PLATE_W * scale, drawnH = PLATE_H * scale;\n  const offX = (frameW - drawnW) / 2, offY = (frameH - drawnH) / 2;\n\n  const qcx = SCREEN_QUAD.reduce((a, p) => a + p[0], 0) / 4;\n  const qcy = SCREEN_QUAD.reduce((a, p) => a + p[1], 0) / 4;\n  const cx = offX + qcx * drawnW, cy = offY + qcy * drawnH;\n\n  // Scaling happens about the frame centre, so the translate needed scales too.\n  const wantX = (frameW / 2 - cx) * zoom * pull;\n  const wantY = (frameH / 2 - cy) * zoom * pull;\n\n  const maxX = Math.max(0, (drawnW * zoom - frameW) / 2 - headroom);\n  const maxY = Math.max(0, (drawnH * zoom - frameH) / 2 - headroom);\n\n  const x = Math.max(-maxX, Math.min(maxX, wantX));\n  const y = Math.max(-maxY, Math.min(maxY, wantY));\n  return { x, y, shortfall: Math.abs(wantX - x) };\n}"}

{"file_path": "C:\\Users\\Joshu\\dev\\ai-os\\projects\\video\\src\\pov\\Room.tsx", "content": "import React from \"react\";\nimport { AbsoluteFill, Img, staticFile, interpolate, Easing } from \"remotion\";\nimport { SEC, LOGICAL_W, LOGICAL_H } from \"../rrc/tokens\";\nimport { quadTransform, screenQuadPx, centringShift } from \"./quad\";\nimport type { Chunk } from \"../rrc/Stimulus\";\nimport type { Seg, Phase } from \"./beats\";\nimport { phaseAt, phaseProgress, returnAt } from \"./beats\";\n\n/*\n * The POV room.\n *\n * Plate, screen and every floating card live inside ONE camera wrapper, so the\n * whole world moves together. That is what makes an annotation read as an\n * object in the room rather than an overlay on the frame.\n *\n * Two rules this file exists to keep:\n *  1. The screen is Remotion, never generated. Kling cannot render the stimulus.\n *  2. NOTHING HIDES. See below.\n *\n * ROUND 6 - the blur is gone.\n *\n * Every chunk he is not reading used to be blurred to 2.6px, and all of them to\n * 3.1px while he spoke. The intent was optical attention. The effect was that\n * the film demonstrated a screen doing the student's looking-away for them, and\n * the drill's own briefing refuses exactly that:\n *\n *   \"While you are working the stimulus, nothing on the screen hides. The whole\n *    thing stays readable, which means the only thing stopping you rereading a\n *    line while you tell it back is you, looking away from it.\"\n *   \"A screen that hides the text for you is a crutch that cannot come with you.\"\n *\n * So attention is now carried two ways, neither of which costs legibility:\n *   - the CAMERA glances off the page, which is the student's own discipline;\n *   - a soft pool of LIGHT travels the line he is reading, which adds emphasis\n *     without taking any away from the rest.\n */\n\n/** How far the eye slides off the text when he looks away, in frame px. */\nconst GLANCE_X = 96;\nconst GLANCE_Y = 26;\n\n/**\n * Zoom, and how much of the off-centre monitor we buy back with it.\n *\n * These two numbers trade against each other and are the whole of the framing.\n * `centringShift` clamps the pan to whatever the zoom affords, so raising PULL\n * past what BASE_ZOOM can pay for does nothing. Fully centring needs zoom 1.45,\n * where the monitor stands 994px tall in a 1080 frame and the room disappears.\n * 1.26 keeps the desk, the window and the wall while moving the monitor from\n * x=654 to x=827 of 1920, which is most of the visible error without turning a\n * POV shot into a screen recording.\n */\nconst BASE_ZOOM = 1.26;\nconst CENTRE_PULL = 1;\n\nconst ease = Easing.bezier(0.4, 0, 0.2, 1);\n\n/** The standing framing offset. Everything below is relative to this. */\nfunction base(frameW: number, frameH: number) {\n  return centringShift(frameW, frameH, BASE_ZOOM, CENTRE_PULL);\n}\n\nexport function cameraAt(\n  s: Seg, t: number, frameW: number, frameH: number,\n): { x: number; y: number; zoom: number } {\n  const p = phaseAt(s, t);\n  const k = phaseProgress(s, t);\n  const b = base(frameW, frameH);\n\n  if (p === \"read\") {\n    // Micro-drift down the Chunk, the way an eye tracks a line. Deliberately\n    // small: this should be felt, not seen.\n    return { x: b.x - 6 * k, y: b.y + 7 * k, zoom: BASE_ZOOM + 0.006 * k };\n  }\n  if (p === \"release\") {\n    // Focus lets go before the eye does.\n    return { x: b.x + interpolate(k, [0, 1], [-6, -26], { easing: ease }),\n             y: b.y + interpolate(k, [0, 1], [7, 12], { easing: ease }),\n             zoom: BASE_ZOOM + 0.006 };\n  }\n  if (p === \"speak\") {\n    // Glanced off. Settles quickly, then hangs with a slow living drift.\n    const settle = interpolate(k, [0, 0.14], [0, 1], { extrapolateRight: \"clamp\", easing: ease });\n    const drift = Math.sin(k * Math.PI * 1.6) * 6;\n    return { x: b.x - 26 - (GLANCE_X - 26) * settle + drift,\n             y: b.y + 12 + (GLANCE_Y - 12) * settle + drift * 0.35,\n             zoom: BASE_ZOOM + 0.006 - 0.012 * settle };\n  }\n  // confirm: the eye comes back to the page\n  const back = interpolate(k, [0, 0.42], [0, 1], { extrapolateRight: \"clamp\", easing: ease });\n  return { x: b.x + interpolate(back, [0, 1], [-GLANCE_X, 0]),\n           y: b.y + interpolate(back, [0, 1], [GLANCE_Y, 0]),\n           zoom: interpolate(back, [0, 1], [BASE_ZOOM - 0.006, BASE_ZOOM]) };\n}\n\n/** Squarely on the screen, nothing panned off it. */\nexport function camNeutral(frameW: number, frameH: number) {\n  const b = base(frameW, frameH);\n  return { x: b.x, y: b.y, zoom: BASE_ZOOM };\n}\n\n/**\n * The review, R6-Q2. He sits back a touch and takes the whole page in.\n *\n * Pulling back rather than pushing in is the point: this is not another read,\n * it is a check that the whole thing is held. The eye covers everything, so the\n * camera has no one place to be.\n */\nexport function camReview(frameW: number, frameH: number, k: number) {\n  const b = base(frameW, frameH);\n  const out = Math.sin(Math.min(1, k) * Math.PI) * 0.024;\n  return { x: b.x, y: b.y - 4 * Math.sin(k * Math.PI), zoom: BASE_ZOOM - out };\n}\n\n/** Where the eye is, as a pool of light travelling the words. */\nexport type ReadLight = {\n  /** Chunk numbers being lit, or \"all\" during the review sweep. */\n  ns: number[] | \"all\";\n  /** 0..1 through the sweep. */\n  p: number;\n  /** Words covered by the pool. Wider for a check than for a read. */\n  spread?: number;\n};\n\n/**\n * The stimulus as the drill renders it: everything readable, always.\n *\n * The only thing added is light. A word is never dimmed, never blurred and\n * never marked; the lit words simply have a warm pool sitting on them, the way\n * a lamp falls on a page. That is what keeps this from reading as an app\n * feature the student will go looking for and not find (Round 1).\n */\nexport const POVStimulus: React.FC<{\n  chunks: Chunk[];\n  light: ReadLight | null;\n}> = ({ chunks, light }) => {\n  // Words are laid out per chunk, but the pool travels the LIT RUN as one\n  // stream, so it crosses a chunk boundary the way an eye does.\n  const litNs: number[] =\n    light === null ? [] :\n    light.ns === \"all\" ? chunks.map((c) => c.n) : light.ns;\n\n  const runWords: { cn: number; wi: number }[] = [];\n  for (const c of chunks) {\n    if (!litNs.includes(c.n)) continue;\n    c.text.trim().split(/\\s+/).forEach((_, wi) => runWords.push({ cn: c.n, wi }));\n  }\n  const centre = light && runWords.length ? light.p * (runWords.length - 1) : -1;\n  const sigma = light?.spread ?? 1.7;\n\n  const heatOf = (cn: number, wi: number): number => {\n    if (centre < 0) return 0;\n    const idx = runWords.findIndex((r) => r.cn === cn && r.wi === wi);\n    if (idx < 0) return 0;\n    const d = (idx - centre) / sigma;\n    return Math.exp(-d * d);\n  };\n\n  return (\n    <div\n      style={{\n        maxWidth: \"52ch\", color: SEC.ink, fontFamily: SEC.serif,\n        fontSize: SEC.stimulusPx, fontWeight: 400, lineHeight: SEC.stimulusLh,\n        fontOpticalSizing: \"none\", fontVariationSettings: '\"opsz\" 14',\n      }}\n    >\n      {chunks.map((c) => (\n        <span key={c.n}>\n          {c.text.trim().split(/\\s+/).map((w, wi) => {\n            const h = heatOf(c.n, wi);\n            const on = h > 0.02;\n            return (\n              <span\n                key={wi}\n                style={on ? {\n                  background: `rgba(255,206,132,${(0.34 * h).toFixed(3)})`,\n                  boxShadow: `0 0 ${(15 * h).toFixed(1)}px ${(8 * h).toFixed(1)}px rgba(255,203,126,${(0.28 * h).toFixed(3)})`,\n                  borderRadius: 5,\n                  color: h > 0.35 ? \"#0a0f1c\" : SEC.ink,\n                } : undefined}\n              >\n                {w}{\" \"}\n              </span>\n            );\n          })}\n        </span>\n      ))}\n    </div>\n  );\n};\n\n/** Composites any screen content onto the monitor in the plate. */\nexport const Monitor: React.FC<{\n  frameW: number; frameH: number; children: React.ReactNode;\n}> = ({ frameW, frameH, children }) => {\n  const [p0, p1, p2, p3] = screenQuadPx(frameW, frameH);\n  return (\n    <div\n      style={{\n        position: \"absolute\", top: 0, left: 0,\n        width: LOGICAL_W, height: LOGICAL_H,\n        transform: quadTransform(LOGICAL_W, LOGICAL_H, p0, p1, p2, p3),\n        transformOrigin: \"0 0\",\n        // The panel is emissive; a touch of light spills past its edges.\n        boxShadow: \"0 0 120px 24px rgba(150,170,255,0.10)\",\n      }}\n    >\n      {children}\n    </div>\n  );\n};\n\nexport const Room: React.FC<{\n  seg: Seg; t: number; frameW: number; frameH: number;\n  /* Beats that are not part of the read/glance loop supply their own camera.\n     During The Recall he is facing a blank screen, not glancing off it, so\n     inheriting the glance-return would crop the app's own footer. */\n  cam?: { x: number; y: number; zoom: number };\n  children: React.ReactNode;\n}> = ({ seg, t, frameW, frameH, cam: camOverride, children }) => {\n  const cam = camOverride ?? cameraAt(seg, t, frameW, frameH);\n  return (\n    <AbsoluteFill style={{ overflow: \"hidden\", background: \"#0b0a10\" }}>\n      <div\n        style={{\n          position: \"absolute\", inset: 0,\n          transform: `translate(${cam.x.toFixed(2)}px, ${cam.y.toFixed(2)}px) scale(${cam.zoom.toFixed(4)})`,\n          transformOrigin: \"50% 50%\",\n        }}\n      >\n        <Img\n          src={staticFile(\"plates/room-v3.png\")}\n          style={{ position: \"absolute\", inset: 0, width: \"100%\", height: \"100%\", objectFit: \"cover\" }}\n        />\n        {children}\n      </div>\n    </AbsoluteFill>\n  );\n};\n\nexport { returnAt };\nexport type { Phase };\n"}
