The briefing is explicit: \"you\n         press it twice per stimulus. The first press takes the text away...\n         The second press moves you to the next stimulus.\" The film pressed once.\n\nOutput per stimulus:\n  public/audio/<slug>.instr.mp3     the built soundtrack\n  public/content/<slug>.script.json the picture timeline, frame-accurate\n\"\"\"\nimport json, io, os, re, subprocess, sys\n\nBASE = os.path.dirname(os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__)))\nTL = json.load(io.open(os.path.join(BASE, \"public/content/timeline.json\"), encoding=\"utf-8\"))\nBOUND = json.load(io.open(os.path.join(BASE, \"align/boundaries.json\"), encoding=\"utf-8\"))\nAUD = os.path.join(BASE, \"public/audio\")\nGEN = os.path.join(BASE, \"recall/generated\")\nTMP = os.path.join(BASE, \"align\", \"_instr\")\nos.makedirs(TMP, exist_ok=True)\n\n# How long each introduction card holds, in seconds.\nHOLD = {\"read\": 5.0, \"tell\": 5.4, \"colour\": 5.6, \"recall\": 6.2}\n# The review, the press, and the beat after the recall.\nREVIEW_HOLD = 5.0\nPRESS_LEAD, PRESS_TAIL = 1.1, 1.4\nPRESS2_HOLD = 1.6\n\nALOUD = \"aecho=0.86:0.9:22:0.10\"\n\n# --- R6-Q8: the four approved edits, in ORIGINAL recording time -------------\n# `patch` is a wav that takes the span's place; None simply removes it.\nEDITS = {\n    \"PT105_S2_Q1\": [\n        (18.24, 21.12, None,\n         \"volcano: unrelated to a flooded sea cave, the one moment a student stops to wonder\"),\n        (35.96, 41.06, \"Q1_TUNNEL.patch.wav\",\n         \"YouTube aside: talks past the material to an audience and dates the film\"),\n        (76.50, 80.18, None,\n         \"stalactite line: outside knowledge he hedges on, reads as the teacher being unsure\"),\n    ],\n    \"PT105_S2_Q3\": [\n        (70.68, 78.40, \"Q3_ASSUMPTION.patch.wav\",\n         \"as recorded it asserts the assumption as fact, the opposite of his point\"),\n    ],\n}\n\n\ndef run(cmd):\n    r = subprocess.run(cmd, shell=True, capture_output=True, text=True)\n    if r.returncode != 0:\n        print(r.stderr[-1200:]); sys.exit(\"FAILED\")\n\n\ndef dur_of(path):\n    return float(subprocess.run(\n        f'ffprobe -v error -show_entries format=duration -of csv=p=0 \"{path}\"',\n        shell=True, capture_output=True, text=True).stdout.strip())\n\n\ndef norm(w):\n    return re.sub(r\"[^a-z0-9]\", \"\", w.lower())\n\n\ndef phrase_at(words, phrase, after, before):\n    \"\"\"First timestamp at or after `after` where `phrase` starts. Measured, never authored.\"\"\"\n    toks = [norm(t) for t in phrase.split() if norm(t)]\n    for i in range(len(words)):\n        if words[i][\"start\"] < after or words[i][\"start\"] > before:\n            continue\n        j, k = i, 0\n        while j < len(words) and k < len(toks):\n            if norm(words[j][\"w\"]) == toks[k]:\n                k += 1\n            elif norm(words[j][\"w\"]) == \"\":\n                pass\n            else:\n                break\n            j += 1\n        if k == len(toks):\n            return words[i][\"start\"]\n    return None\n\n\ndef build(slug, introduce, cue):\n    \"\"\"introduce: step cards to play in full. cue: steps that get a one-line cue.\"\"\"\n    segs = TL[slug][\"segments\"]\n    src = os.path.join(AUD, f\"{slug}.mp3\")\n    recall = os.path.join(GEN, f\"{slug}.recall.wav\")\n    words = json.load(io.open(os.path.join(BASE, f\"align/{slug}.words.json\"), encoding=\"utf-8\"))\n    edits = sorted(EDITS.get(slug, []), key=lambda e: e[0])\n\n    pieces, beats, t = [], [], 0.0\n    cut_total, patch_total = 0.0, 0.0\n\n    def piece():\n        return os.path.join(TMP, f\"{slug}_{len(pieces):03d}.wav\")\n\n    def silence(dur, kind, **extra):\n        nonlocal t\n        p = piece()\n        run(f'ffmpeg -y -v error -f lavfi -i anullsrc=r=48000:cl=mono -t {dur:.3f} \"{p}\"')\n        pieces.append(p)\n        beats.append({\"kind\": kind, \"start\": round(t, 3), \"end\": round(t + dur, 3), **extra})\n        t += dur\n\n    def grab(a, b):\n        \"\"\"Emit source[a,b] with edits applied. Returns the emitted duration.\"\"\"\n        nonlocal cut_total, patch_total\n        out, cursor = 0.0, a\n        for (es, ee, patch, _why) in edits:\n            if ee <= a or es >= b:\n                continue\n            es_c, ee_c = max(es, a), min(ee, b)\n            if es_c > cursor:\n                p = piece()\n                run(f'ffmpeg -y -v error -ss {cursor:.3f} -to {es_c:.3f} -i \"{src}\" '\n                    f'-af \"{ALOUD}\" -ar 48000 -ac 1 \"{p}\"')\n                pieces.append(p); out += es_c - cursor\n            cut_total += ee_c - es_c\n            if patch:\n                pp = os.path.join(GEN, patch)\n                p = piece()\n                # The patch is his cloned voice; it gets the same room echo as\n                # the real recording so the two cannot be told apart by tone.\n                run(f'ffmpeg -y -v error -i \"{pp}\" -af \"{ALOUD}\" -ar 48000 -ac 1 \"{p}\"')\n                pieces.append(p)\n                d = dur_of(p); out += d; patch_total += d\n            cursor = ee_c\n        if cursor < b:\n            p = piece()\n            run(f'ffmpeg -y -v error -ss {cursor:.3f} -to {b:.3f} -i \"{src}\" '\n                f'-af \"{ALOUD}\" -ar 48000 -ac 1 \"{p}\"')\n            pieces.append(p); out += b - cursor\n        return out\n\n    def take(a, b, kind, **extra):\n        nonlocal t\n        d = grab(a, b)\n        # srcStart maps this beat back onto the ORIGINAL recording, so caption\n        # cues (written against his real run) still resolve after teaching beats\n        # have been cut into the timeline. Beats containing an edit are marked\n        # `edited` so the caption layer can decline rather than mistime.\n        touched = any(not (ee <= a or es >= b) for (es, ee, _p, _w) in edits)\n        beats.append({\"kind\": kind, \"start\": round(t, 3), \"end\": round(t + d, 3),\n                      \"srcStart\": round(a, 3), \"edited\": touched, **extra})\n        t += d\n\n    for i, s in enumerate(segs):\n        gi, ns = s[\"gi\"], s[\"chunkNs\"]\n        first = (i == 0)\n\n        # ---- Step 1. Read it. Silent, and exactly as long as he really took.\n        if first and \"read\" in introduce:\n            silence(HOLD[\"read\"], \"card\", step=\"read\")\n        silence(s[\"readEnd\"] - s[\"readStart\"], \"read\", gi=gi, chunkNs=ns,\n                **({\"cue\": \"read\"} if first and \"read\" in cue else {}))\n\n        # ---- The boundary between step 2 and step 3, measured from his run.\n        b = phrase_at(words, BOUND[slug][i], s[\"saidStart\"], s[\"workEnd\"])\n        if b is None:\n            sys.exit(f\"FAILED: boundary not found for {slug} gi={gi}: {BOUND[slug][i]!r}\")\n\n        # ---- Step 2. Tell it back. Begins at the LOOK-AWAY, not at first sound.\n        if first and \"tell\" in introduce:\n            silence(HOLD[\"tell\"], \"card\", step=\"tell\")\n        take(s[\"readEnd\"], b, \"tell\", gi=gi, chunkNs=ns,\n             **({\"cue\": \"tell\"} if first and \"tell\" in cue else {}))\n\n        # ---- Step 3. Colour it.\n        if first and \"colour\" in introduce:\n            silence(HOLD[\"colour\"], \"card\", step=\"colour\")\n        take(b, s[\"workEnd\"], \"colour\", gi=gi, chunkNs=ns,\n             **({\"cue\": \"colour\"} if first and \"colour\" in cue else {}))\n\n    # ---- The review. Silent. Whole page readable, checked once (R6-Q2).\n    silence(REVIEW_HOLD, \"review\", chunkNs=[c for s in segs for c in s[\"chunkNs\"]],\n            **({\"cue\": \"review\"} if \"review\" in cue else {}))\n\n    # ---- The Recall: introduced, the first press, then he gives it back.\n    if \"recall\" in introduce:\n        silence(HOLD[\"recall\"], \"card\", step=\"recall\")\n    silence(PRESS_LEAD, \"press\", phase=\"before\",\n            **({\"cue\": \"recall\"} if \"recall\" in cue else {}))\n    rdur = dur_of(recall)\n    p = piece()\n    run(f'ffmpeg -y -v error -i \"{recall}\" -af \"{ALOUD}\" -ar 48000 -ac 1 \"{p}\"')\n    pieces.append(p)\n    beats.append({\"kind\": \"recall\", \"start\": round(t, 3), \"end\": round(t + rdur, 3)})\n    t += rdur\n    # ---- The SECOND press. The app presses twice per stimulus; the film pressed\n    #      once. \"Press space again and the next one loads.\"\n    silence(PRESS2_HOLD, \"press2\")\n    silence(PRESS_TAIL, \"settle\")\n\n    lst = os.path.join(TMP, f\"{slug}.txt\")\n    with io.open(lst, \"w\", encoding=\"utf-8\") as fh:\n        for p in pieces:\n            fh.write(f\"file '{os.path.basename(p)}'\\n\")\n    voice = os.path.join(TMP, f\"{slug}_voice.wav\")\n    run(f'ffmpeg -y -v error -f concat -safe 0 -i \"{lst}\" -c copy \"{voice}\"')\n\n    # The bed, gated over the silent stretches only, so it carries them.\n    gate = []\n    for b_ in beats:\n        if b_[\"kind\"] in (\"read\", \"review\") and b_[\"end\"] - b_[\"start\"] > 0.6:\n            gate.append(f\"between(t,{b_['start']:.3f},{b_['end']:.3f})*\"\n                        f\"min(1,min((t-{b_['start']:.3f})/0.55,({b_['end']:.3f}-t)/0.5))\")\n    env = \"+\".join(gate) if gate else \"0\"\n    bed = os.path.join(TMP, f\"{slug}_bed.wav\")\n    run(f'ffmpeg -y -v error -f lavfi -i \"sine=frequency=98:duration={t:.3f}:sample_rate=48000\" '\n        f'-f lavfi -i \"sine=frequency=147:duration={t:.3f}:sample_rate=48000\" '\n        f'-filter_complex \"[0:a]volume=0.075[a];[1:a]volume=0.030[b];[a][b]amix=inputs=2:normalize=0,'\n        f'lowpass=f=420,volume=\\'{env}\\':eval=frame[o]\" -map \"[o]\" -ar 48000 -ac 1 \"{bed}\"')\n\n    out = os.path.join(AUD, f\"{slug}.instr.mp3\")\n    run(f'ffmpeg -y -v error -i \"{voice}\" -i \"{bed}\" '\n        f'-filter_complex \"[0:a][1:a]amix=inputs=2:normalize=0:duration=first[o]\" '\n        f'-map \"[o]\" -codec:a libmp3lame -b:a 192k \"{out}\"')\n\n    script = {\"slug\": slug, \"duration\": round(t, 3), \"beats\": beats}\n    json.dump(script, io.open(os.path.join(BASE, f\"public/content/{slug}.script.json\"),\n                              \"w\", encoding=\"utf-8\"), indent=1)\n\n    got = dur_of(out)\n    kinds = {}\n    for b_ in beats:\n        kinds[b_[\"kind\"]] = kinds.get(b_[\"kind\"], 0) + 1\n    tell = sum(b_[\"end\"] - b_[\"start\"] for b_ in beats if b_[\"kind\"] == \"tell\")\n    col = sum(b_[\"end\"] - b_[\"start\"] for b_ in beats if b_[\"kind\"] == \"colour\")\n    print(f\"{slug}: {len(beats)} beats {kinds}\")\n    print(f\"   step2 {tell:5.1f}s | step3 {col:5.1f}s | \"\n          f\"cut {cut_total:4.1f}s | patched in {patch_total:4.1f}s\")\n    print(f\"   script {t:.2f}s | audio {got:.2f}s | \"\n          f\"{'OK' if abs(got - t) < 0.15 else '*** DRIFT ***'} | \"\n          f\"was {TL[slug]['duration']:.2f}s, +{t - TL[slug]['duration']:.1f}s of teaching\")\n\n\n# Q1 introduces every step with a full stop. Q2 and Q3 are already understood,\n# so they get a one-line CUE per step instead of a stop (R6, his answer: \"give a\n# one line for each of these steps so we have a clear understanding of what the\n# recall is\"). Before Round 6 they got nothing at all and The Recall simply\n# arrived.\nbuild(\"PT105_S2_Q1\", {\"read\", \"tell\", \"colour\", \"recall\"}, {\"review\"})\nfor s in (\"PT105_S2_Q2\", \"PT105_S2_Q3\"):\n    build(s, set(), {\"read\", \"tell\", \"colour\", \"review\", \"recall\"})\n"}

import React from "react";
import { Composition } from "remotion";
import { RRC, plan, type Payload } from "./rrc/RRC";
import { POVWorking, type Cue } from "./pov/POVWorking";
import { POVInstructional, type Script } from "./pov/POVInstructional";
import type { Seg } from "./pov/beats";

import stimuli from "../public/content/stimuli.json";
import timeline from "../public/content/timeline.json";
import c1 from "../public/content/PT105_S2_Q1.cues.json";
import c2 from "../public/content/PT105_S2_Q2.cues.json";
import c3 from "../public/content/PT105_S2_Q3.cues.json";
import s1 from "../public/content/PT105_S2_Q1.script.json";
import s2 from "../public/content/PT105_S2_Q2.script.json";
import s3 from "../public/content/PT105_S2_Q3.script.json";

const FPS = 30;
const payload = {
  stimuli: stimuli as Payload["stimuli"],
  timeline: timeline as Payload["timeline"],
  cues: { PT105_S2_Q1: c1, PT105_S2_Q2: c2, PT105_S2_Q3: c3 } as Payload["cues"],
};

/*
 * The three stimuli, with the scaffolding fading across them.
 *
 * Q1 names every step and confirms what he carried. Q2 still confirms but no
 * longer labels it. By Q3 nothing explains the run at all. That fade is the
 * lesson: it is how the skill has to end up in her hands.
 *
 * The spoken-word cards do NOT fade. They are also the captions, and this
 * journey scored 2/5 on accessibility in the 2026-09-09 audit.
 */
const CUES = { PT105_S2_Q1: c1, PT105_S2_Q2: c2, PT105_S2_Q3: c3 } as Record<string, Cue[]>;

const POV = [
  { id: "POVQ1", slug: "PT105_S2_Q1", qIndex: 1, scaffold: 2 as const },
  { id: "POVQ2", slug: "PT105_S2_Q2", qIndex: 2, scaffold: 1 as const },
  { id: "POVQ3", slug: "PT105_S2_Q3", qIndex: 3, scaffold: 0 as const },
];

/* The instructional cut. Steps named and introduced, reading genuinely silent,
   and The Recall demonstrated. Duration comes from the beat script, not the mp3,
   because the encoder pads the file and that padding lands in trailing silence. */
const SCRIPTS: Record<string, Script> = {
  PT105_S2_Q1: s1 as Script,
  PT105_S2_Q2: s2 as Script,
  PT105_S2_Q3: s3 as Script,
};

export const RemotionRoot: React.FC = () => (
  <>
    <Composition
      id="RRCWalkthrough"
      component={RRC as never}
      durationInFrames={plan(payload.timeline, FPS).total}
      fps={FPS}
      width={1920}
      height={1080}
      defaultProps={payload as never}
    />

    {/* The POV cut, one composition per stimulus. Duration is always the real
        audio length from the aligned timeline, never a guessed number. */}
    {POV.map(({ id, slug, qIndex, scaffold }) => {
      const tl = (timeline as Payload["timeline"])[slug];
      return (
        <Composition
          key={id}
          id={id}
          component={POVWorking as never}
          durationInFrames={Math.round(tl.duration * FPS)}
          fps={FPS}
          width={1920}
          height={1080}
          defaultProps={{
            slug,
            chunks: (stimuli as Payload["stimuli"])[slug].chunks,
            segments: tl.segments as unknown as Seg[],
            cues: CUES[slug],
            audioSrc: `audio/${slug}.pov.mp3`,
            where: "PT105 Section 2",
            qIndex,
            qTotal: 23,
            scaffold,
          } as never}
        />
      );
    })}

    {POV.map(({ slug, qIndex }) => {
      const sc = SCRIPTS[slug];
      return (
        <Composition
          key={`INSTR${qIndex}`}
          id={`InstrQ${qIndex}`}
          component={POVInstructional as never}
          durationInFrames={Math.round(sc.duration * FPS)}
          fps={FPS}
          width={1920}
          height={1080}
          defaultProps={{
            slug,
            chunks: (stimuli as Payload["stimuli"])[slug].chunks,
            script: sc,
            cues: CUES[slug],
            audioSrc: `audio/${slug}.instr.mp3`,
            where: "PT105 Section 2",
            qIndex,
            qTotal: 23,
          } as never}
        />
      );
    })}
  </>
);

The map is what lets captions survive an edit:\n        each entry says that `d` seconds starting `o` into this beat came from\n        source time `s`, or from a patch (`s` null, `text` set). Without it, a\n        caption lookup after a cut is wrong by the length of the cut.\n        \"\"\"\n        nonlocal cut_total, patch_total\n        out, cursor, m = 0.0, a, []\n        for (es, ee, patch, _why) in edits:\n            if ee <= a or es >= b:\n                continue\n            es_c, ee_c = max(es, a), min(ee, b)\n            if es_c > cursor:\n                d = slice_src(cursor, es_c)\n                m.append({\"o\": round(out, 3), \"s\": round(cursor, 3), \"d\": round(d, 3)})\n                out += d\n            cut_total += ee_c - es_c\n            if patch:\n                pp = os.path.join(GEN, patch)\n                p = piece()\n                # The patch is his cloned voice; it gets the same room echo as\n                # the real recording so the two cannot be told apart by tone.\n                run(f'ffmpeg -y -v error -i \"{pp}\" -af \"{ALOUD}\" -ar 48000 -ac 1 \"{p}\"')\n                pieces.append(p)\n                d = dur_of(p)\n                m.append({\"o\": round(out, 3), \"s\": None, \"d\": round(d, 3),\n                          \"text\": PATCH_TEXT.get(patch, \"\")})\n                out += d; patch_total += d\n            cursor = ee_c\n        if cursor < b:\n            d = slice_src(cursor, b)\n            m.append({\"o\": round(out, 3), \"s\": round(cursor, 3), \"d\": round(d, 3)})\n            out += d\n        return out, m\n\n    def take(a, b, kind, **extra):\n        nonlocal t\n        d, m = grab(a, b)\n        # srcStart maps this beat back onto the ORIGINAL recording, so caption\n        # cues (written against his real run) still resolve after teaching beats\n        # have been cut into the timeline. When the beat contains an edit, the\n        # single offset is not enough and `map` carries the piecewise mapping.\n        entry = {\"kind\": kind, \"start\": round(t, 3), \"end\": round(t + d, 3),\n                 \"srcStart\": round(a, 3), **extra}\n        if len(m) > 1:\n            entry[\"map\"] = m\n        beats.append(entry)\n        t += d"}

{"file_path": "C:\\Users\\Joshu\\dev\\ai-os\\projects\\video\\src\\pov\\POVInstructional.tsx", "content": "import React from \"react\";\nimport { AbsoluteFill, Audio, staticFile, useCurrentFrame, useVideoConfig, interpolate } from \"remotion\";\nimport { Screen } from \"../rrc/Screen\";\nimport type { Chunk } from \"../rrc/Stimulus\";\nimport { Room, Monitor, POVStimulus, camNeutral, camReview, type ReadLight } from \"./Room\";\nimport { SpeechBubble, enterAt } from \"./Float\";\nimport { StepCard, StepLabel, StepCue, type StepKey } from \"./Instruction\";\nimport { RecallStack, RecallProgress, stackLeave, type RecallSentence } from \"./RecallStack\";\nimport type { Seg } from \"./beats\";\n\nexport type Cue = { start: number; end: number; text: string };\n\n/** One piece of a beat's audio, and where it came from. See build_script.grab. */\nexport type BeatMap = { o: number; s: number | null; d: number; text?: string };\n\nexport type Beat = {\n  kind: \"card\" | \"read\" | \"tell\" | \"colour\" | \"review\" | \"press\" | \"recall\" | \"press2\" | \"settle\";\n  start: number;\n  end: number;\n  step?: StepKey;\n  cue?: StepKey;\n  gi?: number;\n  chunkNs?: number[];\n  /* Where this beat sits in the ORIGINAL recording, so caption cues still\n     resolve once teaching beats have been cut into the timeline. */\n  srcStart?: number;\n  /* Present only when the beat contains an edit, where one offset is not\n     enough. Each entry maps a stretch of this beat back to source time, or to\n     a synthesised patch carrying its own text. */\n  map?: BeatMap[];\n  phase?: string;\n};\n\nexport type Script = { slug: string; duration: number; beats: Beat[] };\n\nconst mmss = (t: number) =>\n  `${Math.floor(t / 60)}:${String(Math.floor(t % 60)).padStart(2, \"0\")}`;\n\n/*\n * The instructional cut.\n *\n * Driven entirely by the beat script that align/build_script.py emitted\n * alongside the soundtrack, so picture and audio cannot drift.\n *\n *   card     the picture holds and a step is introduced. His audio is silent.\n *   read     SILENT. A pool of light travels the words at his real reading pace.\n *   tell     step 2. Begins at the LOOK-AWAY, which is why the camera glances\n *            off here rather than at first sound.\n *   colour   step 3. Same glance, continuing: tell and colour share one camera\n *            move, or the cut would jolt at the boundary between them.\n *   review   step 4's lead-in. Silent, whole page lit through once as a CHECK.\n *   press    the app's own Space chip depresses. Text goes.\n *   recall   the real drill exactly, with the argument rebuilding in the room.\n *   press2   the second press. \"Press space again and the next one loads.\"\n */\nexport const POVInstructional: React.FC<{\n  slug: string;\n  chunks: Chunk[];\n  script: Script;\n  cues: Cue[];\n  recall: RecallSentence[];\n  audioSrc: string;\n  where: string;\n  qIndex: number;\n  qTotal: number;\n}> = ({ chunks, script, cues, recall, audioSrc, where, qIndex, qTotal }) => {\n  const frame = useCurrentFrame();\n  const { fps, width, height, durationInFrames } = useVideoConfig();\n  const t = frame / fps;\n\n  const beat = script.beats.find((b) => t >= b.start && t < b.end) ?? script.beats[script.beats.length - 1];\n  const k = beat.end > beat.start ? (t - beat.start) / (beat.end - beat.start) : 1;\n\n  // Which step is on screen. Cards name themselves; the rest map from the beat.\n  const stepOf: Partial<Record<Beat[\"kind\"], StepKey>> = {\n    read: \"read\", tell: \"tell\", colour: \"colour\", review: \"review\",\n    press: \"recall\", recall: \"recall\", press2: \"recall\",\n  };\n  const step: StepKey | null = beat.kind === \"card\" ? beat.step! : (stepOf[beat.kind] ?? null);\n\n  // A step is only labelled once its card has played. Where there are no cards\n  // at all (Q2, Q3) the label carries from the first frame, and a one-line cue\n  // rides under it the first time each step happens.\n  const introduced = new Set(\n    script.beats.filter((b) => b.kind === \"card\" && b.start < t).map((b) => b.step!),\n  );\n  const anyCards = script.beats.some((b) => b.kind === \"card\");\n  const labelOn = step !== null && beat.kind !== \"card\" && (!anyCards || introduced.has(step));\n\n  // Frames since this beat began, for springing the label in.\n  const beatFrames = Math.round((t - beat.start) * fps);\n\n  // The stimulus is gone from the press onward, exactly as the drill does it.\n  const recalling = beat.kind === \"recall\" || beat.kind === \"settle\" || beat.kind === \"press2\" ||\n    (beat.kind === \"press\" && k > 0.55);\n  const pressAnim = (kk: number) =>\n    interpolate(kk, [0.5, 0.58, 0.72, 0.8], [0, 1, 1, 0], { extrapolateLeft: \"clamp\", extrapolateRight: \"clamp\" });\n  const pressed =\n    beat.kind === \"press\" ? pressAnim(k) :\n    beat.kind === \"press2\" ? pressAnim(k) : 0;\n\n  /*\n   * Captions. Lookup runs in ORIGINAL recording time, because the cues were\n   * written against his real run. `map` handles beats containing an edit: a\n   * plain srcStart offset would be wrong by the length of the cut for every\n   * word after it, and would show the words he no longer says.\n   */\n  let srcT = -1;\n  let patchText: string | null = null;\n  if (beat.map) {\n    const into = t - beat.start;\n    for (const m of beat.map) {\n      if (into >= m.o && into < m.o + m.d) {\n        if (m.s === null) patchText = m.text ?? null;\n        else srcT = m.s + (into - m.o);\n        break;\n      }\n    }\n  } else if (beat.srcStart !== undefined) {\n    srcT = beat.srcStart + (t - beat.start);\n  }\n  const cueIdx = srcT >= 0 ? cues.findIndex((c) => srcT >= c.start && srcT < c.end) : -1;\n  const cue = cueIdx >= 0 ? cues[cueIdx] : null;\n  const speaking = beat.kind === \"tell\" || beat.kind === \"colour\";\n  const bubbleText = patchText ?? (cue ? cue.text : null);\n  const showBubble = speaking && !!bubbleText;\n  // Springs from the start of the patch, or from the start of the cue.\n  const bubbleFrom = patchText !== null\n    ? Math.round((t - beat.start) * fps)\n    : cue ? Math.round((srcT - cue.start) * fps) : 0;\n\n  /*\n   * The camera runs off a synthetic segment built from the beats of THIS gi, so\n   * read -> tell -> colour is one continuous glance. Building it per beat would\n   * re-settle the glance at the tell/colour boundary, which would just move the\n   * glitch rather than fix it.\n   */\n  const gi = beat.gi;\n  const readB = script.beats.find((b) => b.kind === \"read\" && b.gi === gi);\n  const tellB = script.beats.find((b) => b.kind === \"tell\" && b.gi === gi);\n  const colB = script.beats.find((b) => b.kind === \"colour\" && b.gi === gi);\n  const activeNs = beat.chunkNs ?? [];\n  const seg: Seg = {\n    gi: gi ?? 0, chunkNs: activeNs,\n    readStart: readB?.start ?? beat.start,\n    readEnd: readB?.end ?? beat.start,\n    saidStart: tellB?.start ?? beat.start,\n    workEnd: colB?.end ?? beat.end,\n    readCoverage: 1,\n  };\n\n  /*\n   * The reading light. Only ever on while he is READING or CHECKING - never\n   * while he is talking, because during those he has looked away and there is\n   * no eye on the page to show.\n   */\n  const light: ReadLight | null =\n    beat.kind === \"read\" ? { ns: activeNs, p: k, spread: 1.7 } :\n    beat.kind === \"review\" ? { ns: \"all\", p: Math.min(1, k * 1.06), spread: 3.4 } :\n    null;\n\n  const cam =\n    beat.kind === \"review\" ? camReview(width, height, k) :\n    [\"press\", \"recall\", \"settle\", \"press2\", \"card\"].includes(beat.kind) ? camNeutral(width, height) :\n    undefined;\n\n  // The Recall, rebuilding in the room.\n  const recallB = script.beats.find((b) => b.kind === \"recall\");\n  const inRecall = beat.kind === \"recall\" || beat.kind === \"press2\" || beat.kind === \"settle\";\n  const recallT = recallB ? t - recallB.start : -1;\n  const shownCount = recallB\n    ? recall.filter((s) => s.text.trim().split(/\\s+/).length >= 3 && recallT >= s.start).length\n    : 0;\n  const totalCount = recall.filter((s) => s.text.trim().split(/\\s+/).length >= 3).length;\n  const stackOn = inRecall && recallT >= 0\n    ? (beat.kind === \"recall\" ? stackLeave(2) : 1) : 0;\n\n  const through = Math.min(1, frame / durationInFrames);\n\n  return (\n    <AbsoluteFill>\n      <Audio src={staticFile(audioSrc)} />\n\n      <Room seg={seg} t={t} frameW={width} frameH={height} cam={cam}>\n        <Monitor frameW={width} frameH={height}>\n          <Screen\n            through={through}\n            clock={mmss(t)}\n            where={`${where}  ·  ${qIndex} of ${qTotal}`}\n            instruction={recalling\n              ? \"Say the whole argument back from memory.\"\n              : \"Read it. Tell it back and colour it in as you go.\"}\n            keyDoes={recalling ? \"next stimulus\" : \"recall full stimulus\"}\n            pressed={pressed}\n          >\n            {recalling ? null : <POVStimulus chunks={chunks} light={light} />}\n          </Screen>\n        </Monitor>\n\n        {inRecall && recallT >= 0 && stackOn > 0 && (\n          <div style={{ opacity: stackOn }}>\n            <RecallStack sentences={recall} t={recallT} fps={fps} />\n            <RecallProgress done={shownCount} total={totalCount} enter={stackOn} />\n          </div>\n        )}\n\n        {showBubble && bubbleText && (\n          <SpeechBubble\n            key={patchText !== null ? `patch-${beat.start}` : cueIdx}\n            text={bubbleText}\n            enter={enterAt(bubbleFrom, fps)}\n            x={1120}\n            y={392}\n          />\n        )}\n\n        {labelOn && step && (\n          <>\n            <StepLabel step={step} enter={enterAt(beatFrames, fps)} />\n            {beat.cue && <StepCue step={beat.cue} enter={enterAt(beatFrames - 8, fps)} />}\n          </>\n        )}\n      </Room>\n\n      {beat.kind === \"card\" && beat.step && <StepCard step={beat.step} k={k} />}\n    </AbsoluteFill>\n  );\n};\n"}

Scale is chosen from the FULL line count, not the\n   * count so far, or the stack would visibly shrink under itself as it grows.\n   */\n  const budget = 1.0 + lines.reduce((a, s) => a + Math.ceil(s.text.length / 46) * 0.5, 0);\n  const fit = Math.min(1, 4.6 / budget);\n  const size = Math.max(19, Math.round(30 * fit));\n\n  return (\n    <div\n      style={{\n        position: \"absolute\", left: 232, top: 150, width: 1180,\n        perspective: 1150, perspectiveOrigin: \"42% 46%\", zIndex: 6,\n      }}\n    >", "new_string": "  /*\n   * Everything said so far stays up, so the column has to fit the WHOLE argument\n   * by the end. Size is therefore chosen from the full line count, never the\n   * count so far, or the stack would visibly shrink under itself as it grew.\n   *\n   * Chosen by trying sizes rather than by a fudge factor: the number of lines a\n   * sentence wraps to depends on the size, and the height depends on the lines,\n   * so a single formula gets it wrong in both directions. Q1's third sentence is\n   * 291 characters and was the case that broke the guess.\n   */\n  const AVAIL = 500;   // room-space px between the stack top and the page bottom\n  const WIDTH = 1180;\n  let size = 30, gap = 11;\n  for (; size > 17; size--) {\n    const cpl = Math.max(20, Math.floor(WIDTH / (size * 0.52)));\n    const h = lines.reduce(\n      (a, s) => a + Math.ceil(s.text.length / cpl) * size * 1.34 + 18 + gap, 0);\n    if (h <= AVAIL) break;\n  }\n  const fit = size / 30;\n\n  return (\n    <div\n      style={{\n        position: \"absolute\", left: 232, top: 290, width: WIDTH,\n        perspective: 1150, perspectiveOrigin: \"42% 46%\", zIndex: 6,\n      }}\n    >"}

Over blank paper, a tilted glass card with its own shadow can\n * only be read as hanging in front of the screen.\n *\n * `place=\"top\"` is for The Recall, where the page is blank and the rebuilt\n * argument occupies the middle: the label goes above the stack instead.\n */\nexport const StepLabel: React.FC<{ step: StepKey; enter: number; place?: \"page\" | \"top\" }> = ({\n  step, enter, place = \"page\",\n}) => {\n  const s = STEPS[step];\n  const y = place === \"top\" ? 178 : 548;\n  return (\n    <Plate3D x={430} y={y} w={s.n === null ? 372 : 352} tiltX={6} tiltY={9} rgb=\"150,170,255\" enter={enter} depth={17}>\"\"\")\nt=t.replace(\"\"\"export const StepCue: React.FC<{ step: StepKey; enter: number }> = ({ step, enter }) => {\n  const s = STEPS[step];\n  return (\n    <Plate3D x={318} y={172} w={556} tiltX={5} tiltY={8} rgb=\"150,170,255\" enter={enter} depth={13}>\"\"\",\n\"\"\"export const StepCue: React.FC<{ step: StepKey; enter: number; place?: \"page\" | \"top\" }> = ({\n  step, enter, place = \"page\",\n}) => {\n  const s = STEPS[step];\n  const y = place === \"top\" ? 258 : 628;\n  return (\n    <Plate3D x={430} y={y} w={556} tiltX={5} tiltY={8} rgb=\"150,170,255\" enter={enter} depth={13}>\"\"\")\nio.open(p2,'w',encoding='utf-8').write(t)\nprint(\"patched\")\nPY\ngrep -n \"place ===\" src/pov/Instruction.tsx", "description": "Reposition label and fix stack fade"}

Tool result:
# LSAT U video

Remotion project for LSAT U instructional video. Verified rendering 2026-09-09.

## The RRC walkthrough

`RRCWalkthrough` shows a real run of PT105 Section 2, questions 1 to 3, with
Joshua's own model-run recordings as the audio and a teaching overlay on top.

```bash
npm run dev                                  # studio
npx remotion bundle                          # build the bundle first
npx remotion render build RRCWalkthrough out/rrc-walkthrough.mp4
```

Render from a **built bundle**, not the default temp path. The temp-directory
render hits an ENOENT on this machine; bundling to `build/` first sidesteps it.

## Two machine-specific gotchas

- **rspack emits no JavaScript here.** `Config.setRspack(true)` (which
  `create-video` writes when you accept Tailwind) prints "Bundling 100%", exits
  0, and produces only `index.html`. The resulting `ENOENT ... bundle.js` is a
  decoy: it is the source-map reader failing, not the real fault. It is
  commented out in `remotion.config.ts`. Do not re-enable without testing a render.
- **npm blocks install scripts**, so esbuild's binary never installs. Fix with
  `npm approve-scripts esbuild`.

## How the overlay stays in sync

1. `align/transcribe.py` runs faster-whisper over the three MP3s for word-level
   timestamps. Agreement with the stored Deepgram transcripts is 96.8 to 98.0%.
2. `align/timeline.js` aligns `walkthrough_md` to that word stream with global
   Needleman-Wunsch, giving the exact span where each Chunk is read aloud.
   Fuzzy anchoring was tried first and drifted; do not go back to it.
3. `align/copycheck.js` checks on-screen copy against the banned vocabulary in
   `activity-centre/CONTEXT.md`.

Colours and type in `src/rrc/tokens.ts` are sampled from the real
`recall/section.css`. Re-sample rather than eyeball if it changes.

**Layout law:** the teaching layer owns a 96px band across the top and never
overlaps the replicated app. The first cut floated it over the app's header rail
and clipped it, which is exactly the confusion the design forbids.

## The audio is generated, not recorded

Round 3 re-voices the whole film with Kokoro-82M using his exact words. See
`audio/README.md` for the run order, the four verification gates and the four
Windows/ffmpeg traps found building it. `public/content/audio-timeline.json`
supersedes `public/content/timeline.json` for playback: because the audio is
generated from known text, every boundary in it is measured off a file rather
than inferred, and word timings verify at 100% instead of 91-97%.

`public/content/timeline.json` is still the STRUCTURAL source. It is where his
real grouping of Chunks comes from, and `audio/build-script.mjs` reads it.

## Known gap: The Recall - CLOSED

The model runs contain steps 1 to 3 (Read, Tell it back, Colouring In) and The

## assistant

Tool call: Bash
{"command": "cd \"C:/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os/projects/video\" && cat >> README.md <<'EOF'\n\n## Round 6 (2026-09-10): the instructional cut, corrected against the app\n\nThe film had drifted from the drill it teaches. Every change below traces to\n`read-recall-colour-section.instructions.json` in the LSAT U repo or to a\ndecision captured in `brainstorms/2026-09-10-rrc-instructional-video-round6.md`.\n\n**The rule that would have prevented most of it:** never paraphrase a\nstudent-facing string that already exists in the app. Go and read it. Two of the\nfour step labels were inventions (\"Reading\", \"Colouring In\"); the app says\n\"Read it.\" and \"Colour it.\", and its `briefing.sections[1].steps[].short` lines\nare already second-person imperatives written in his voice.\n\n**Nothing hides.** `chunkBlur` is gone. The briefing is explicit that the whole\nstimulus stays readable and that looking away is the student's own discipline:\n\"a screen that hides the text for you is a crutch that cannot come with you.\"\nThe old blur demonstrated exactly that crutch. Attention is now carried by the\ncamera glance plus a warm pool of light travelling the words at his real reading\npace - light ADDS emphasis without taking legibility away, which is why it is\ncompatible where blur was not.\n\n**Steps 2 and 3 are split at a measured boundary.** The old cut labelled the\n0.4s breath between reading and speaking \"Tell it back\" and all 17-34s of speech\n\"Colouring In\", so step 2 flashed on and off inside half a second. Step 2 is the\nfirst part of what he SAYS. Boundaries live in `align/boundaries.json` as\nphrases and resolve against `align/<slug>.words.json`, so the timing is measured\nfrom his run. **Phrases must match the Whisper stream, not `walkthroughs.json`** -\nthe two differ (\"Like if you have an infection\" vs \"Like, you have an infection\").\n\n**The camera compensates for an off-centre plate.** `centringShift` in\n`quad.ts`. The detected screen quad spans plate x 0.071-0.616, so the monitor's\ncentroid sits at x=0.34 and the camera never accounted for it. Panning right\npulls the plate's left edge toward frame, so how far you can pan is capped by\nzoom: `maxShift = (drawnW * zoom - frameW) / 2`. Full centring needs zoom >= 1.45,\nwhere the monitor stands 994px tall in a 1080 frame and the room disappears. At\n1.26 the monitor centre moves 654 -> 825 of 1920 and the room survives. The\nfunction CLAMPS and reports `shortfall` rather than silently under-delivering.\n\n**Audio edits.** `EDITS` in `align/build_script.py`, spec in `recall/patches.json`,\nreplacement voice from `recall/generate_patches.py` (same Chatterbox path as the\nrecall passages). Two traps:\n\n- **Measure every emitted piece, never assume its nominal length.** An ffmpeg\n  `-ss/-to` slice lands on a frame boundary, so a slice is never exactly `b-a`.\n  Assuming it put picture 0.22s off audio by the end of Q1, which splices most.\n- **A cue is a caption line written against the UNEDITED run.** A cue straddling\n  a cut still contains the removed words - Q1's \"like a volcano situation. Who\n  knows?\" is one, and the first half is exactly what came out. ## user

Base directory for this skill: C:\Users\Joshu\.claude\skills\session-handoff

# Session Handoff

Produce a repeatable end-of-session summary so Joshua can `/clear` and start a fresh agent without losing continuity. The next agent should pick up by reading this summary alone.

This is a **context-handoff artifact**, not a status report. The audience is a future instance of you, not a stakeholder.

**Handoff vs. wrapup:** run `session-handoff` when the *context window* is the problem — nearing ~250k tokens (the dumb zone), or passing work between Claude and Codex. It is chat-only and writes nothing. Run `wrapup` when the *work day* is done — it strikes tasks, refreshes `wiki/hot.md`, and appends `wiki/log.md`. They can be run back to back (wrapup to persist, then handoff to reset).

## When to invoke

Joshua says: "session handoff", "hand off", "handoff summary", "summarize before I clear", or any near-equivalent. Note "let's wrap up" belongs to `wrapup`, not here — if he says that and the context window is also deep, run `wrapup` first, then offer this. Also invoke proactively if he says he's about to `/clear` without having run it, or if the session is clearly deep into the dumb zone.

## How to produce the summary

1. **Review the full conversation**, not just the last few turns. Handoffs miss things when they only summarize recent context.
2. **Pull state from these sources (in order):**
   - Plan files referenced this session (check `~/.claude/plans/` if a plan was mentioned).
   - TodoWrite state — any in-progress or pending tasks.
   - Background processes started with `run_in_background` — shell IDs are load-bearing for the next agent.
   - Files created or modified this session — you know what you touched; don't grep to re-discover.
   - Memory files written or updated (`~/.claude/projects/C--Users-Joshu-dev-ai-os/memory/`).
   - Unresolved questions — things you asked Joshua that never got a clear answer, or things he asked that got deflected.
3. **Do NOT audit the filesystem.** This is synthesis of what happened in THIS session. No `git log`, no broad `Glob` sweeps. If you didn't touch it this session, it doesn't belong here.
4. **Produce the output in chat.** Do not write a file. Do not update memory. Chat-only.

## Output template — use exactly this structure, every time

```
# Session Handoff — <one-line title of what this session was about>

## Where it started
<2-3 sentences: what Joshua asked for, key framing or constraints that emerged>

## Decisions locked + what shipped
- <decision or change> — <why, and where it lives (absolute path if a file)>
- ...

## Key files for next session
- `<absolute path>` — <why the next agent should read this first>
- Plan file: `<path>` (if a plan drove the session)
- Memory files touched: `<paths>` (if any)

## Running state
- Background processes: <shell IDs + what they are + how to kill> — or "none"
- Dev servers / ports: <url + port> — or "none"
- Open worktrees / branches: <paths> — or "none"

## Verified state as of <HH:MM>
- <claim about live/system state> — <how it was checked, and when>
- <claim> — NOT re-verified this session; last confirmed <when>
- ...

## Verification — how to confirm things still work
- `<command>` — <expected outcome>
- ...

## Deferred + open questions
- Deferred: <item> — <why pushed to later>
- OPEN (must be resolved or re-asked before new work): <question needing Joshua's input> — <context>

## Pick up here
<1-2 sentences: the single most likely next action for a fresh agent>
```

## Hard rules

1. **Chat output only.** Never write the handoff to a file. Never update memory from this skill. (That is `wrapup`'s job, not this one.)
2. **Never invent state.** If a section has nothing to report, write "none" — do not omit the section. Structure stability is the whole point.
3. **Absolute paths always.** The next agent may have a different working directory, or be Codex.
4. **If a plan file drove the session, name it first** in "Key files" so the next agent reads it before anything else.
5. **No emojis, no hype, no "great job" summaries.** Terse and concrete — paths, commands, shell IDs, decisions. Tone of a seasoned engineer handing off at end-of-shift.
6. **Background process IDs are critical.** If you started any `run_in_background` shells, their IDs must appear in "Running state" with the kill command — the next agent cannot find them otherwise.
7. **Stamp verified state, and separate it from assumed state.** Run `date "+%H:%M"` and use the real time. Every claim about live systems (deploy status, DB contents, what was sent, what is published) is either something you actually checked this session — say how — or explicitly marked NOT re-verified. A handoff that presents stale belief as current state makes the next session re-derive everything, which is the exact cost this skill exists to remove.
8. **Open questions are obligations, not notes.** Anything under OPEN must be resolved by the next session or re-asked to Joshua before it starts new work. Requirements have been silently re-typed across sessions, and a promised artifact once never arrived because nobody re-asked. If a question was open in the handoff you are picking up from, it is the first thing you raise.

## Anti-patterns — do not do these

- Summarizing the last 3 turns and calling it a handoff.
- Listing files by relative path.
- Skipping "Running state" because "nothing is running" — write "none" instead.
- Writing the summary to any file. This is chat-only by design.
- Adding a "what went well / what went poorly" retrospective. This isn't a retro.
- Recommending next steps beyond the single "Pick up here" line. The next agent decides; you just hand off.

# Session Handoff — RRC instructional video, Round 6: corrected against the app's own drill spec

## Where it started
Picked up from a Round 5 handoff whose instruction was to wait for his reaction to `instr-q1.mp4` and raise two open labelling questions. He answered with a full revision list (not instructional, glitches between colouring and retelling, cut what goes too far, read-along light, screen off-centre, more 3D/first-person, one line per step in Q2/Q3, mine the HyperFrames kit), then stopped the build mid-turn to say the film was missing a step and to go review the real RRC drill before continuing. Grounding in `read-recall-colour-section.instructions.json` invalidated several shipped decisions, so a Round 6 grill was run and the film rebuilt.

## Decisions locked + what shipped
- **App copy is the source of truth for step vocabulary** — two of four labels were inventions.
